# antseal MVP — Adaptive TODO

**Source of truth for scope**: `MVP-SPEC.md` (Revision 2, 2026-07-27 — frozen; `SPEC-REVIEW.md` already folded in). When this list and the spec disagree, the spec wins; flag the conflict, don't silently diverge.
**Source of truth for status**: this file. Full per-task detail (Do / Accept / Notes / spec line refs) lives in `tasks/{P,F,C,G,S,A,R,U,Q}.md` — one file per domain, task IDs are stable and permanent.

Generated 2026-07-27 by a 9-agent decomposition of the spec + 2-agent adversarial coverage/consistency verification, then grown by the waves that execute it: **394 tasks** today (219 at generation; discovered work takes the next free ID in its domain and is never renumbered), **91 decisions D1–D91** of which **78 are resolved**, 6 milestone gates. Counts below are current, not original.

## How to use this list (the adaptive protocol)

1. **Statuses** on the checkbox lines here (never in `tasks/*.md`):
   - `- [ ]` todo · `- [x]` done (append `✅ YYYY-MM-DD`) · append `⏳ WIP` while in progress · append `⛔ <blocker>` when blocked.
   - Never delete a task: strike it through (`~~…~~`) with a one-line reason and date. Renumbering is forbidden — IDs are referenced everywhere.
2. **Discovering new work**: take the next free number in the owning domain (e.g. `F18`), write the full entry (Milestone/Size/Deps/Spec/Do/Accept) in `tasks/<domain>.md`, add its checkbox line under the owning milestone here.
3. **Decisions**: when a Decision-register entry (below) is resolved, check it, record the outcome + date inline, and update the blocked tasks' detail entries. Decisions are as first-class as tasks — an unresolved decision past its due milestone is a blocker.
4. **Milestone gates**: a milestone is done only when its gate checklist passes — the gate, not the task count, is the exit criterion. Run the gate, record evidence, then move the Current-focus block forward.
5. **Re-verification triggers** (adaptive part — these mutate the list):
   - any `ant-core`/`self_encryption` bump → re-run S1 survey + S9 pinning + devnet E2E; adapter changes confined per S20.
   - any RUSTSEC advisory on a pinned crypto crate → P13/Q10 lane red → decision entry + possible new tasks.
   - upstream weekly check (P19/Q36) findings → new tasks under the affected domain.
   - any spec revision → re-derive the affected section's tasks before coding against it.
6. **Milestone fidelity**: spec-assigned milestones are normative. Where a milestone was inferred (mostly U; noted per task), it may move with a recorded reason; spec-assigned ones may not.
7. **Deps discipline**: Deps lines in `tasks/*.md` list true predecessors only; consumer/interface mentions belong in Notes or carry an explicit "consumer, not a dep" annotation. Where prose and this file disagree on ordering, **the `after:` lists on the checkbox lines here are the authoritative build order** (verified acyclic).

## Current focus

> **Phase: M2 — Anchors, IN PROGRESS. 34 of 79 rows done.** M1 is complete (its gate passed on the integrated tree). M2's planning round ran first, as the house protocol requires, and resolved **all nine of D53–D61 plus D90 and D91, both minted mid-wave** from gaps the work itself exposed — D90 because `tasks/A.md` A3 depended on an HTTP-client pin that no task and no decision owned, D91 because two decisions resolved the *same day* specified two different error-code prefixes and two different codes for one check.
> **Done and merged**: A2 (data model) · A3+P29+A49 (the `ureq =3.3.0` substrate) · A5+A8 (strict DER + CMS over **nine real TSAs**) · A11+A12 (in-house `.ots` codec, the crate rejected outright) · A13/A14/A15 (calendar clients, polling, opportunistic engine) · A4/A16/A17/A24/A42 (request builder, must-agree esplora, Arbitrum two-RPC, mocks) · A6/A7/A9/A26/A43 (pinned root store, four roots admitted, X.509 path validation) · A10/A20/A31/A59 (capture client, **pre-payment gate**, TSA identity, signing mock) · A23/Q17 (fuzz targets) · A38/A41/A52/Q73/Q77 (the 54-code registration) · Q74/Q76/Q80/Q81/Q82/Q83/Q84 (CI and contract guards).
> **The wave's dominant finding is about instruments, not code.** **Six** test instruments were caught unable to report red: a stub serving connections serially, an `--exact` filter matching no test while libtest exited 0, a mutation harness whose verdict parser never matched cargo's output, a `cargo check` over modules that were never declared, a lane's tests silently contacting the real calendars, and `anchor_token` **fuzzing with no seeds since A5** because a `[ -d … ] &&` lookup failed silently. Related and sharper: the root-store lane's suite passed **25/25 while four of eleven wrong implementations survived it**, because real fixtures offer one viable path and outlive their children — a green suite over real material can be silent about the quantifier a ruling actually makes.
> **Product defects caught, each of which would have shipped**: D90's timeout ladder would have **retried a delivered calendar POST** (three pending attestations where two are asserted); the nonce comparison's two operands were encoded differently, so **half of all real captures would have been rejected** and aborted before payment; a path builder walking the supplied chain to its end anchors on a **cross-certificate**, rendering the production DigiCert default `internally-consistent-only` — and A24's single-hierarchy test CA can never reach it; and a bare 404 read from esplora would have turned an operator typo into a **forgery accusation**, because D56's O7 makes an agreed absence *refute* an anchor.
> **The two-day OTS estimate was wrong and is corrected in three documents**: the cycle took **13 h 47 m**. Six upgraded `.ots` are committed. Measured en route: the three calendars committed to **three different Bitcoin blocks**, so A12's "at least one commits" is load-bearing — requiring all three would reject an honest artifact.
> **Next, in order**: **U22** wires A20's gate into the product — it is proven to abort before payment *in tests* while `seal_run.rs` still passes `&NoAnchorGate` and `seal_plan.rs` still refuses every anchored seal, which is exactly the recorded-guarantee-the-product-lacks shape M1 hit three times. Then **A18/A19** (the state machine, in flight), **R12** (wire the anchor stage into `verify_bundle`), **A21**'s **eight** tamper rows (not seven — D53's recount), **U23–U26**, **Q16/Q18**, and finally **A22**, the gate's exit criterion. **A25's blocking wall-clock item is discharged**; its writeup remains.
> **Maintainer actions** (all external, none taken): (1) **P2** `cargo login` + `scripts/reserve-crates.sh --execute`; (2) **P3** register antseal.org + antseal.dev; (3) **P17's funding step** — per **D38** no faucet, mint or bridge exists on Arbitrum Sepolia, so test-ANT can only arrive as an ERC-20 transfer from an existing holder; the preflight, env surface and runbook are committed and the paid-operation Accept row is ⛔ maintainer (it does **not** block M1, which runs entirely on the local devnet); (4) **push** — the M1 work is **local only**; the last push was 2026-08-01 (`d3345e1..62ea9d4`) and any further push needs express in-the-moment consent per the standing rule; (5) **branch protection** remains ⛔ BLOCKED BY PLAN (403 on a private repo on GitHub Free), which is itself part of D52's reasoning.

## Critical path

P1 → P4/P5/P6/P8 → **M0**: [P10+F1 CBOR pin] → F2→F3→F4/F5→F6→F7 · C11 probe → C12/C13→C14 · G1→G2 · C1/C2→C6 · G8→G9→G11→G12→G13 → R1→R5 → vectors (F12/F13, C16, G15, R9) → F14/Q11 independent cross-check → **Q14 format-v1 freeze** → **M1**: S2→S3/S4/S6 · U5→U6→U9 · S12/U13 pipeline → S17/S18/S19 E2E → **M2**: A5→A8→A9 · A11→A13→A14 · A18 → A21/A22 + A25 smoke · U22 gate → **M3**: R13→R16 · R17→R18 · R22→R23→R25→R26 · R27 → **M4**: Q30→Q31→Q32→Q33→**Q34 release**.

The M0 freeze (Q14) is the single most consequential gate: everything frozen there (formats, tags, labels, Unicode version, context string, sig-policy IDs) is permanent once the first real seal exists.

Two independent layers (coreutils `sha256sum -c` compatible + a Rust checker), self-tests first each run. Rule 3 uses Cargo.lock's own encoding — a dep entry is version-qualified iff the package resolves to >1 version, so `evmlib` listing a bare `"alloy"` **is** the lockstep statement — plus family-wide single-versioning and the recorded `=` pin literal; all three branches proven red against a doctored lock. Documented in `docs/dependency-policy.md` §1 as one containment story

Rows: 8 barriers, D37's mid-sub-batch kill (each sub-batch paid once, map complete, no hash paid twice), the four D36 rows incl. pay-arg identity and a stale-quote-never-paid row, D49's dry-run row, partial-store remainder, abandon-on-missing-bytes, and a 24-case proptest over kill/resume interleavings (all terminate complete-or-abandoned, none double-pays; no counterexample, so no shrink fixture). "Never re-encrypted" is not sampled — it is structural (resume holds no `W`)
- [x] **S29** (S) **A `vault import`ed complete work cannot be restored — the manifest is unlocatable.** U12's export applies D43 §3's cache exclusion to the *whole* journal area (`export.rs`: `state == Complete ⇒ journal = []`, and the import validator hard-errors if a complete work carries any journal bytes), so a complete work loses entry 0 (state), entry 1 (the plaintext manifest) and entry 2 (the encrypted manifest's `{address, nonce}`) — and an encrypted manifest cannot be found on a content-addressed network without its address, which is not derivable from `W` or `work_id`. S14's engine has both manifest paths and neither has a source after import; S19's clean-tree drill therefore cannot pass, and M3 `reveal`-after-import has the same hole (R16 needs the manifest too). D43 §3's *reasoning* is entirely about content scale ("record scale (KBs)" vs "1–2× sealed content"), which the plan record is not — so the exclusion looks over-broad by accident rather than by decision. Recommended fix (one line each side): scope the exclusion to the staged-blob keys (`≥ MANIFEST_BLOB_ENTRY`), exporting entries 0–1 for complete works; the alternative is a `{manifest_address, manifest_nonce}` pair in U9's always-exported meta record. Either is a D43/D47 amendment + an export-format review, so it was **not** taken unilaterally by lane ε — after S14,U12 + D43/D47 amendment · discovered by S14 (lane ε) 2026-08-02 ✅ 2026-08-02 — scoped to the staged **unit** blobs (`>= UNIT_ENTRY_BASE`) on both sides of the format: `gather_payload` filters, `validate_payload` rejects only unit keys, so a complete work exports its record-scale head `[0,1,2]` and an imported work has **both** of S14's manifest sources back. **Deviation from the recorded one-liner, deliberate**: the entry said `>= MANIFEST_BLOB_ENTRY`, which is off by one against its own diagnosis — that keeps 0–1 and still drops entry 2, the `{address, nonce}` the diagnosis names as the missing locator. It would have "worked" via the plaintext copy alone, leaving the network path with no source at all. Proven by `an_imported_complete_work_restores_from_the_network`: seal → export → wipe → import → restore byte-identical with `from_cache == 0` on every file (so every unit really came off the wire), then the plaintext copy deleted and restore repeated — `ManifestSource::Network`, which is entry 2's locator doing the work it exists for. U12's size arithmetic still holds; its fixture was corrected to span both halves of the namespace, having modelled every complete-work entry as content-scale, which was never true. Dated amendments at source: **D43** (§3's over-broad category, the corrected rule "the cache is the staged unit blobs, not the journal area", why entry 2 ships whole rather than as a stripped pair, and the honestly-narrowed "pure network path with zero test contrivance" claim) and **D47** (payload rule restated at entry-key level; `EXPORT_FORMAT_VERSION` stays 1 — schema unchanged, only the writer's selection widens, both compat directions checked — plus a new residual risk: pre-fix backups import cleanly and stay unrestorable forever, since the locator was never written). S19's clean-tree drill unblocked; M3's `reveal`-after-import (R16) had the identical hole and is fixed with it
- [x] **S27** (S) Nothing forces the seal pipeline's backend constructor to install D37's per-sub-batch capture hook (`AntCoreBackend::with_capture_hook`), and without it a crash in the post-pay/pre-receipt-journal window costs a **second payment** — S16 asserts both directions of exactly that. The hook is what makes the window empty; wiring it must be a checked obligation of U13/S17's construction path, not a convention — after S6,S12,S16 · discovered by S16 (lane ι) 2026-08-02 ✅ 2026-08-02 — **closed by S31, in three parts, and only the third made the entry's own sentence true.** (1) *Cannot be forgotten*: U36 made `SealBackend::connect` the only expression in the crate that can produce a backend (private module, private field, sink a required argument, no `Default`/builder/second constructor) and welded it shut with an **ungated** source scan proving the bypass type `AntCoreBackend` is nameable in exactly one file — proven red against a planted mention. (2) *Actually installed on the real path*: S17 asserted the hook fires on a live `pay` against a devnet, which the type system cannot say. (3) *And now actually useful*: before S31 the hook was installed and its only production sink dropped the receipt, so "the hook is what makes the window empty" was **false even with it installed** — S31's `VaultReceiptSink` is what makes it true, proven live in both directions (a `SIGKILL` between sub-batch txs costs one tx per sub-batch with it, and re-pays without it). **What is left is a different obligation and is S36, not this one**: the sink must still be *attached* to the journal by the caller, and U13's `seal` wiring — the first production caller that constructs the pairing at all — is where that gets the same two-layer treatment. Leaving S27 open pointing at U13 would have been two rows for one obligation
- [x] **S17** (L) M1 E2E on devnet via library APIs: multi-file --split seal, restore, UNANCHORED verify, --live — after S6,S12–S15 + P16 + R5 ✅ 2026-08-02 — 3 tests green on a live **14-node** devnet (Anvil 1.5.1, chain 31337; boot 6.4 s, happy path 26.6 s, suite 56.5 s; 7 blobs / 6 units / 82031250000000000 atto-ANT / 1 payment tx); registry row flipped `pending → live`. The address equality stops being a tautology here — manifest = journal = independent `compute_storage_address` over the staged ciphertext = **ant-core's own `finalize_batch` assignment** (S4's deferred accept row); spend checked against the consented quote **and** the on-chain ANT balance delta read from Anvil; **S27's live half discharged** (the capture hook is asserted to have fired on a real `pay`). Restore proven off-the-wire (`cache_served_units == 0`, BOM+CRLF file through its raw mirror), `--live` `AllPersisted` over all 7 blobs with a negative control. Deviations: the `backend` seam widened `pub(crate)` → `pub` (D34 puts the E2E in a separate crate; private-field guarantee untouched); seeds are **run-scoped, not constant** (a constant seed re-seals already-stored ciphertext, making the payment assertion vacuously `0 == 0`, and would reuse `(k_u, nonce)`); Accept row 2's grep is a real test over every `tests/e2e_*.rs`. **Fixed at source**: `secret-guard` fired on the live `.devnet/env`, making the two required local lanes mutually exclusive — `.devnet` now excluded by directory like `target`, welded to a new "is it still gitignored" assertion
- [x] **S18** (L) M1 kill/resume matrix on devnet: pay/finalize kill, mid-upload kill, changed-source abandon, real SIGKILL — after S11,S16,S17 ✅ 2026-08-02 — 7 tests green on a live **14-node** devnet in **183.1 s**; registry row flipped `pending → live`. Every payment claim settled **on-chain** (ANT balance delta + `eth_getTransactionReceipt`), never from a receipt the code under test wrote. case (1) barrier: killed at `post-receipt-journal-pre-finalize`, resumed, delta = quote **once** (58593750000000000 atto-ANT, 1 tx). case (1) **real SIGKILL**: child killed with state `Paid` on disk, reaped in 3 ms, resumed without paying — the row a barrier cannot cover, since a barrier returns `Err` from a function whose journal write already completed and so can never falsify its atomicity. case (1b): forced cap 1 → 5 non-zero transfers → **5 sub-batch txs, 5 hook captures, 5 tx_map entries**, no hash repeated. case (2) **real SIGKILL mid-finalize** (state at crash `Finalizing`): all 5 addresses fetched and byte-equal to their staged ciphertexts, nonces unchanged, staging never re-entered. case (3): `StagedBytes(Missing)` + `Abandoned` + zero ANT moved + fresh seal's nonces disjoint from the journaled ones; and with staged bytes intact the resume restores the **original**, not the edited sources. Deviations: `SealBackend::with_max_transfers_per_tx` added (a `with_` modifier, not a second constructor — `connect` is still the only way to make one); S17's M3 scan narrowed from a blanket `process::Command` ban to "a spawner must resolve its program from `current_exe`", because accept row 5 *requires* a killable process and `fork` needs denied `unsafe`. **Discovered: S31**
- [x] **S31** (M) **No journal-backed `ReceiptSink` exists**, so D37's per-sub-batch durability has a seam but no implementation — after S27,S12,D37 · discovered by S18 (lane M1 GATE) 2026-08-02 ✅ 2026-08-02 — **option (a)**, and **(b) refuted on a fact the register's own framing missed**: the hook fires in exactly the right place (`ant_backend.rs:906-920` captures at the END of sub-batch N's iteration; `:816` submits N+1 at the start of the next), but it is a **synchronous** `Fn` called inline from `pay`'s body while the pipeline's future is suspended at `backend.pay(quote).await` **in the same task** — so a channel `send` returns with nobody able to dequeue it, and a `select!` drain is polled only when `pay` yields, whose next yield is *inside* `send_transaction` for tx N+1. (b) narrows the window and never closes it; making it close needs a blocking hook plus a drainer thread holding the journal behind a lock, i.e. (a) with extra machinery and a hang-after-money-moved failure mode. `pipeline::VaultReceiptSink` — `Arc<UnlockedVault>` (one key, one zeroize, `UnlockedVault` still `!Clone`), one `Mutex`, U9's atomic+fsync `put_receipt` **inline**, so the hook returns only once the receipt-so-far is on disk. Deliberately **ungated**, with the `impl ReceiptSink` bridge in `backend.rs` the only feature-gated part — the body of D37's one durability guarantee is compiled and tested by the *default* required lane, which is S30's gap and D89's precedent. The sink's **target** is structural: `SealJournal::arm_receipts` (provided no-op, `VaultJournal` overrides) is called by the pipeline immediately before **every** `pay`. It never acquires the U5 lock — U5 refuses a same-process double-acquire by design, so a sink that tried would deadlock the command against itself. **"The row is small once the sink exists" was wrong, and the discovery is the interesting half**: a durable partial receipt stops the double payment but cannot finish the seal — `finalize_batch` refuses a receipt with no record for an unstored blob — so **D37 Decision 5 is extended to the primary flow's unpaid remainder** (re-quote the uncovered blobs *only*, consent over that quote alone, pay, journal the **merge**; the proofs-expired path deliberately does not merge). A **third** correction is the one that would have cost money: a remainder payment is itself a multi-tx payment, so its own captures were **overwriting** the durable prior — a second crash would have stranded everything the first paid for. `arm_receipts` now carries the prior and every capture writes the merge, pinned by a **double-crash** row and proven red against the planted defect. A second correction fell out of the live run: **"no payment record" is not "must be bought"** — an `AlreadyStored` blob is never paid for and never gets a record, and `finalize_batch` skips it on the same evidence, so the *quote* decides what is left to buy, not the receipt. **Live, both directions, 14-node devnet**: green — child pid 750758 SIGKILLed **inside `pay`** after 2/5 sub-batch txs (state at crash `Anchored`, partial receipt durable = 23437500000000000 atto-ANT, reaped in 2 ms), resume bought **only the 3 remaining**, **5 txs on Anvil = one per sub-batch**, tx_map 5 entries, delta 58593750000000000 atto-ANT **once**, 38.3 s. Red — same `SIGKILL` with a non-durable sink: nothing journaled, 23437500000000000 stranded, resume bought all 5 again, **82031250000000000 spent vs 58593750000000000 accounted = exactly the 2 stranded sub-batches lost**, 35.7 s. S18 is now **9 tests / 261.7 s**; the full registry gate reads **PASS, 4 suites, 622 s**. Mock mirrors in the default lane: 6 sink tests + 4 resume tests (incl. the same red direction over `MockBackend`). **Fixed at source**: all four child scenarios shared one seal seed, so every child after the first re-sealed byte-identical ciphertexts at identical addresses and found the whole work `AlreadyStored` — the mid-`pay` children had nothing to be killed between, and **`case2`'s "mid-upload" kill had been silently running over an all-already-stored finalize**. One seed per scenario; case2's runtime went 15.9 s → 32.9 s because it now genuinely uploads. Discovered: **S36** (the sink must still be *attached* by the caller — U13's wiring), **S37** (a failed sink write is recorded, not surfaced)

- [x] **S36** (S) **The durable receipt sink still has to be *attached* by the caller.** S31 made the sink's *target* structural — the pipeline calls `SealJournal::arm_receipts` immediately before every `pay`, on every path that can pay — but `VaultJournal::with_receipt_sink` is an ordinary builder call that can simply be omitted, and a journal without one behaves exactly as the tree did before S31: the hook fires, nothing durable happens, and a crash between sub-batch txs re-pays. This is S27 one level up, with the same shape and the same fix available: U36 discharged "the hook cannot be forgotten" with a private constructor **plus** an ungated source scan proving the bypass type is nameable in one file. Do the same for the pairing — make a paying journal impossible to build without a sink (a constructor that takes it, or a newtype the paying paths require), with a scan that fails when a `VaultJournal::new` on a paying path has no sink. Lands with U13's `seal` wiring, which is the first production caller that constructs the pairing at all (today only the M1 devnet harness does) — after S31,U13 · discovered by S31 (lane m1-s31) 2026-08-02 ✅ 2026-08-02 — **and the premise was too kind: the sink had already been forgotten.** U13's `seal` wiring landed the same day with an in-memory `SealReceipts` for the backend and a bare `VaultJournal::new` for the pipeline, so from `08e501d` a real `antseal seal` had the **pre-S31** behaviour while the suites had the guarantee. Measured, not inferred: reverting `seal_run.rs`'s one line back to the bare journal makes the new devnet row fail on its FIRST assertion — no durable receipt at all. **The ownership question is answered at the point of unlock**: `SealSession::open` takes the `UnlockedVault` **by value** and `Arc`s it, so the `'static` sink shares the one instance — `UnlockedVault` stays `!Clone`, one `VaultKey`, one zeroize, pinned by a `strong_count` row that fails at 3. No leak, no secret clone. **Two layers, U36's template**: (1) `run_seal` takes `&SealSession` and `SealSession::journal` is the only journal source, which always attaches the sink — the paying command has no expression that builds a sinkless one; (2) two ungated source scans pin `VaultJournal::new(` **and** `VaultReceiptSink::new(` to exactly `seal_session.rs`, the second closing the residual the type layer cannot see (a *second* sink means the backend fires one object while the pipeline arms another). Both proven red — a synthetic tree with a planted violation, a legitimate test-module use that must NOT be flagged and a non-`.rs` file; plus the real thing planted in `seal_run.rs`. A `PayingJournal` bound on `Pipeline::seal`/`resume` was rejected on evidence, not taste: the red-direction rows that prove the sink does anything are built out of exactly the sinkless pairing such a bound would make unrepresentable. `SealReceipts` **deleted** (its stated reason — the backend predates the drawn `seal_id` — was answered by S31's `arm_receipts`), taking its two wrong **U39** citations with it; the obligation was U40/S36. **Live, both directions through `run_seal`** on a 14-node devnet, with the resume a SECOND `seal` over the same argv so D45 detection is part of the evidence: green — child pid 874670 SIGKILLed **inside `pay`** of a real `antseal seal` after 2/5 sub-batch txs (state `Anchored`, partial receipt durable = 23437500000000000 atto-ANT, reaped in 5 ms), resume bought **only the 3 remaining**, **5 txs on Anvil = one per sub-batch**, tx_map 5 entries, delta 58593750000000000 atto-ANT **once**, 29.6 s. Red — the same invocation with a sink the pipeline never arms: 23437500000000000 stranded, all 5 sub-batches bought again, **82031250000000000 spent vs 58593750000000000 accounted**, 29.3 s. Suite 9 → **11 tests / 259.9 s**; full registry gate PASS, 4 suites. **Found at base: the entire M1 devnet gate had not compiled since `b775e02`** — `tests/common/devnet.rs` names `antseal_net::evm::WalletKey`, which U37/D89 demoted to a private `use`, and the gate command in use builds lib+bin only while the suites *skip* rather than fail. Fixed here; **U39's second and more expensive instance**. **U40 closes with this** (same obligation, recorded from the command's side)

- [ ] **S37** (S) **A failed sink write is recorded but never surfaced.** `VaultReceiptSink::capture` runs between two transactions on the far side of money having moved, so it must not panic: an encode or I/O failure lands in the sink's fault slot and a `tracing::error!`, and nothing reads it. When `pay` then *succeeds* it is moot — the pipeline's own post-`pay` `put_receipt` writes the complete receipt anyway. When `pay` *fails*, it is the one case that matters: the partial receipt may be lost and the caller gets a bare `StrandedPayment` that does not mention that the record of what DID land could not be written, which is exactly the difference between "resume will finish this" and "resume will pay again". Wire the fault into the pipeline's error path (a `SealJournal` provided method mirroring `arm_receipts` is the obvious shape). Deliberately not taken inside S31: it costs a distinct `SealError` variant, i.e. an error-universe event (194), and adding one inside a lane whose subject is payment durability is how a taxonomy grows by accident — after S31 · discovered by S31 (lane m1-s31) 2026-08-02

Decide and record: accept (Linux-only by nature, local tier 2 is required when touched), a path-filtered PR lane (but D52 E1/E4 argue against both required and non-required per-PR here), or a compile-only heavy pass folded into the weekly lane — after S22,Q15 · discovered by S22 2026-08-02

- [x] **U36** (M) **The CLI has no storage-backend construction seam**, so no command can actually reach Autonomi. Three things have to be joined and none of them is a single command's business: S6's `AntCoreBackend::connect(&NetworkConfig, &WalletKey)` sits behind the non-default `ant-backend` feature (S22's gate); it is `async` over ant-core's tokio client while `antseal-cli` declares no runtime (the dep-graph lane keeps the *normal* graph free of async/network crates, so an optional runtime under the feature is a dependency-policy event); and the constructed backend **must** carry D37's per-sub-batch capture hook or a post-pay crash costs a second payment (S27). `seal` (U13) and `restore` (U20) need the identical object, so it is one function: `crate::backend` declares it and documents the three obligations, U13/S17 fill it. Until then every network-touching command refuses there with a specific message rather than implying an outage. — after U13,S6,S17,S22,S27 · discovered by U20 (lane ε) 2026-08-02 ✅ 2026-08-02 — the seam is built, and **S27's obligation is discharged by the type system rather than by remembering**. `AntCoreBackend` is a builder whose `with_capture_hook` can simply be forgotten; `SealBackend` cannot be. It lives in a private module behind a private field, so the only expression in the crate that produces one is `SealBackend::connect(config, wallet_key, receipts)` — the sink is an ordinary required argument, there is no `Default`, no builder and no second constructor, and `connect` installs D37's hook itself. Two layers, because the type system alone does not close the *bypass*: the raw `AntCoreBackend` is public and satisfies `StorageBackend` on its own, so a hurried command could name it directly, compile, pay, crash and pay again — an **ungated** source scan asserts it is nameable in exactly one file, and the detector was proven red against a planted mention (`left: ["backend.rs", "rng.rs"]`) before its verdict was believed. Commands hold the wrapper and reach the network through its four delegating `StorageBackend` methods. **Deviation on the hook's argument**: it takes a `ReceiptSink` trait object, not `&VaultJournal` — upstream's `CaptureHook` is `'static + Send + Sync` and the journal handle is none of the three (it borrows the unlocked vault and `RefCell`s the RNG); the journal *write* stays the pipeline's and this is only D37's timing seam, which is what the type already said. Proven to deliver **every** capture, not just the first. `ReadOnly` lets the never-paying commands through the same door with "this path does not pay" written down instead of omitted. tokio lands per `docs/dependency-policy.md`: `optional = true`, activated only by `ant-backend`, exactly `devnet-launcher`'s shape — **the lockfile delta is one line** (tokio added to this package; zero new packages), dep-graph still measures 120 default packages, and the runtime is proven to drive a real timer. **Deviation on the §1 row**: §1 is *the exact-pin class* and tokio fails its own membership test — nothing it produces is hashed, signed, stored or paid for — while "one tokio in the tree, the same one ant-core resolves" is exactly what a competing `=` requirement here would break; recorded as a dated §1 note saying why it stays caret + lockfile, with the workspace comment (which claimed tokio was for devnet-launcher **ONLY**) corrected to enumerate all three edges. Also lands the vault→payment-key bridge (U10's 32 bytes → hex → D44's single acceptance gate, refusal carrying no byte of the input) and a two-armed refusal message, because the old one-armed text becomes a lie in a build that *has* the feature. **Discovered en route and fixed at source**: U3's registered `--json` restore fixture hand-copied the refusal string instead of rendering it, so the committed snapshot documented text no build emitted the moment the message changed — `backend` is now `pub` so the fixture calls the real producer (U19's "rendered by the real renderer" rule, one snapshot line re-blessed). 4 tests

- [x] **U40** (S) **D37's capture hook cannot be journal-backed as the seam currently stands** — S27's remaining half, now concrete. `SealBackend::connect` requires a `ReceiptSink` by signature (U36 discharged the *forgetting* half), but the backend is constructed **before** `Pipeline::seal` draws the `seal_id`, so a sink has no work-record key to write a receipt into and `seal`'s production sink (`seal_run::SealReceipts`) is in-memory. The receipt that matters is durable through the pipeline's own `put_receipt` immediately after `pay` returns; the residual window is a payment split across **more than one transaction** (>256 blobs, `MAX_TRANSFERS_PER_TRANSACTION`) crashing between sub-batches, which loses the partial receipt and costs a second payment on resume — exactly the direction S16 asserts. What was missing was the *attachment* — and the fix is the pipeline-side seam this entry predicted, already built. `SealSession` (new, ungated) mints the vault `Arc` and the one `VaultReceiptSink` together; `commands::seal` hands `session.receipts()` to `SealBackend::connect` and `run_seal` takes the session, whose `journal()` cannot omit the sink. `SealReceipts` is **deleted** with its `warn!` and its two wrong U39 citations. **The residual window was NOT >256-blob-only in practice**: the forced cap reaches it with five blobs, and a live devnet row shows a real `antseal seal` stranding 23437500000000000 atto-ANT and re-buying all five sub-batches before this landed — 82031250000000000 spent against 58593750000000000 the completed seal accounts for. S17/S18/S19 do not exist yet, so they are **declared `pending`** in the script's suite registry: a missing suite is a loud `PENDING … DISCHARGES NO GATE` (never PASS, never red), a suite that lands while its row still says pending is a **hard failure**, and a `live` row whose suite vanished is a hard failure — all self-tested, no devnet needed. Today the gate really runs S6-S8's `devnet_backend` suite against a booted devnet; log/manifest capture is redacted because the scheduled lane uploads it. **Accept row 1 ("runs S's full M1 suite green") stays open until S17-S19 land**, at which point their commits move the registry rows and switch on `--require-suites`. Deviations: weekly not daily (this lane must not take `Swatinem/rust-cache` — D52 E3 — so every run is cold), no measurement claimed (D52 §4's deliberate inversion), no full boot executed in this lane. - [x] **A10** (M) TSA HTTP capture client with immediate in-core verification (only verifying tokens count toward the gate) — after A3,A4,A8,A9 ✅ 2026-08-05 — capture client running the **full offline pipeline a bundle recipient runs** (A8's `verify_token` with the nonce, then A9's chain validation to a *pinned* root); a token counts only if it is headline-eligible, never if it merely returned 200. Red twin: **GlobalSign answers 200, is granted, passes every CMS check, chains to an unpinned root, and does not pass.** **Found: the nonce comparison's two operands were encoded differently** — the drawn value is 8 raw bytes, the canonical form prepends `0x00` when the top bit is set, so **half of all real captures would have been rejected** and aborted pre-payment. The FreeTSA fixture cannot catch it; Sectigo's can → A68
- [x] **A11** (M) `.ots` codec wrapper: strict limits, op execution, digest-commitment check — after A2,P18 ✅ 2026-08-02 — in-house `.ots` codec, `opentimestamps` adopted in **no** form, **+0 packages**. All four D58-measured defects are regression tests, three of which the crate could not have *failed* because it **aborts**. Iterative, witnessed at `MAX_OTS_DEPTH` on a 256 KiB stack; no shift by a runtime value, latched by a file-set-checked scan because a CI with no release lane cannot carry that claim in a unit test. Op executor validated against a **live** calendar. Found D58 demanding a code its own §9.4 makes unreachable, and D58's normative depths wrong under D58's own definition (67/12, not 69/13)
- [x] **A12** (S) Embedded Bitcoin header offline check → `attested` (never headline-eligible offline) — after A11 ✅ 2026-08-02 — merkle root read at 36..68 with **no** reversal, offsets asserted at compile time; `header_commits` requires height **and** ops-derived root; `verified_time_unix` is `None` for `attested` **by construction**. - [x] **A13** (M) OTS calendar submission client (≥2 calendars, merged pending `.ots`) — after A3,A11 ✅ 2026-08-03 — submission client + container writer, **byte-exact**: assembling from the three real replies reproduces the committed `merged-A.ots` (664 B) and `merged-B.ots` (629 B) — two independent pieces of code from the same inputs. Per-response validation runs the **real** `parse_ots` on a one-branch container so a bad calendar is attributed to itself; `Complete`/`Thin`/`Absent` counted over **distinct normalised pending URIs**, never HTTP 200s. Found: a wrong-digest response is **accepted at submit time and no local check can see it** — the digest lives in the container header, this client writes it, and an op stream executes from any starting value, so detection is deferred to the calendar's 404, which is why that arm must exist
- [x] **A14** (L) OTS upgrade polling + attestation merge + upgrade-time header embed (must-agree fetched) — after A11–A13,A16 ✅ 2026-08-03 — three-way body-separated discriminator plus a **fourth** observed case (catallaxy answers *nothing* to a corrupted commitment where alice/bob return 404 + 9-byte `Not found`; it stays re-pollable and is never promoted to the hard error). Merge is a **pure byte insertion** re-validated through `parse_ots` — nothing here parses, because this crate must not grow a second `.ots` parser: the pending attestation's wire bytes are *constructed* from the URI the one parser reported, and the splice is refused unless the byte-search count equals the parser's count, which is what makes k-th-occurrence matching sound when D54's aliases put one calendar in twice. Header fetched via A16's must-agree pair and checked with A12's `header_commits` **before** anything is recorded
- [x] **A15** (M) Opportunistic upgrade engine (bounded budget, never fails host command) + `status` data backend — after A14 ✅ 2026-08-03 — engine returns a report and **no `Result`**: there is no error path for a caller to propagate. **Hook placement is forced, not preferred** — the opportunistic global is 3 s and cannot go lower (D90 measured alice at 1.772 s TTFB, so a 1 s budget reports *healthy* calendars as failures), and at 3 s a pre-output hook makes `antseal list` sit silent before printing data it already holds. No timeout fixes that: the delay is not the timeout being wrong, it is the work being ordered before the output. After the flush the same 3 s bounds *lateness of exit* rather than *latency of answer*. CLI wiring is **U48**

Absent is a *state*, not a failure: every vault predating the slot and every fresh one has no file, so `load` returns the default. Decode is total over adversarial input and a newer record version is its own class. The D42 splice matrix extends by one row (a blob moved into or out of the slot fails authentication). One rule worth reading twice: **an import always ends with at least one recorded export**, even from a payload carrying none — reaching that line means an export file was just consumed, and a vault restored from a backup must not greet its owner with "NO BACKUP YET" while they are holding the thing. 4 acceptance + 5 unit tests. Vault-global bookkeeping record slot: new cipher record class + store slot + D47 payload key — the machinery U18's export-performed flag needs and U9/U12 deliberately did not ship — after U9,U12; land with or before U18 (discovered 2026-08-01 by U12's payload design: the store has no vault-global non-wallet record, and the v1 export schema has no bookkeeping key; pre-release the payload may extend within format v1 while both sides move together — post-release it is a version bump, recorded in vault/export.rs)
