## Decision register

Unresolved decisions are blockers-in-waiting: each must land by its due milestone. On resolution: check it, record outcome + date, update the blocked tasks in `tasks/*.md`. (Merged from all nine domains; joint owners shown.)

### Due pre-M0
- [x] **D1** Product name: `antseal` with upstream written blessing vs non-ant rename (P — blocks P2, P3, C12 context string, F4 format strings, U1 binary/dir; gates the M0 freeze) — **Resolved 2026-07-27: `antseal`, maintainer-confirmed FINAL** (third path — no upstream blessing; deviation + residual risk recorded; blessing request downgraded to optional courtesy; `sealstone` fallback retired; availability re-verified at confirmation: 5 crates FREE + dry-run green, .org/.dev FREE). C12 `"antseal-manifest-v1"`, F4 format strings, U1 binary/vault-dir now UNBLOCKED on the name (frozen at Q14). History: provisional 2026-07-27 → deadline re-anchored (request unfiled at M0 start) → maintainer confirmation same day (docs/decisions/D1-product-name.md)
- [x] **D2** Repo hosting platform + CI provider (assumption: GitHub + Actions) (P — blocks P4, P8) — **Resolved 2026-07-27**: GitHub + GitHub Actions; private repo `aed900/antseal` (docs/decisions/D2-hosting-ci.md; hosting re-created under the maintainer account and all commit identities normalized to it before first push — see the D2 amendment)
- [x] **D3** Repo root layout: `code0/` as workspace root vs `antseal/` subdir per the spec tree (P — blocks P4, P5) — **Resolved 2026-07-27**: repo root = workspace root = the spec tree's `antseal/`; no nested subdir (docs/decisions/D3-repo-layout.md)
- [x] **D4** Rust edition + MSRV policy (P — blocks P6) — **Resolved 2026-07-27**: edition 2024; toolchain pinned =1.92.0; MSRV = pinned toolchain, moves only via P7 bump procedure (docs/decisions/D4-edition-msrv.md)
- [x] **D5** CLI crate publish name: spec's `antseal-cli` vs bare `antseal` for `cargo install` (P — reserve both in P2; recorded spec-disagreement) — **Resolved 2026-07-27** as recorded deferral: spec tree stays normative (crate `antseal-cli`, binary `antseal`); both names reserved at P2; publish-name call at Q31 (docs/decisions/D5-cli-crate-name.md)
- [x] **D6** License choice — spec milestone is M4 (Q29) but P2's crates.io placeholders need a license: decide early, execute at M4 (Q/P) — **Resolved 2026-07-27**: per-crate — antseal-core/anchor/verifier-web `MIT OR Apache-2.0`; antseal-net/cli own code dual-licensed but distribution effectively GPL-3.0 while ant-core's mandatory `self_encryption` dep stays GPL-3.0; ⚠ P15 direct-dep plan would pull GPL into the permissive core/WASM page — options recorded, decide at P15/S4; files land Q29 (docs/decisions/D6-license.md) — **2026-07-27 (S1 finding)**: GPL exposure in ant-core's graph is TWO crates — `self_encryption` 0.36.0 AND `evmlib` 0.9.0 (ant-core itself MIT/Apache-2.0); P15/S4 assessment must cover both

### Due M0 (format-freeze relevant — permanent once frozen)
- [x] **D7** Deterministic-CBOR crate + exact pin; in-house-codec contingency trigger (P10/F1) — **Resolved 2026-07-27**: `minicbor = "=2.3.0"`, features `alloc` only, derive off; strict rejections live in F3 on native probes; contingency trigger recorded (docs/decisions/D7-cbor-crate.md)
- [x] **D8** Complete v1 wire registry: key assignments, reserved ranges, signatures container, anchor-status enum values, byte-range representation, integer time encoding, explicit file_id in touched-file entries (F4, with C/G/A/S) — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28** (docs/decisions/D8-wire-registry-final.md), implemented at **F4**, frozen at Q14. All ten §13 feed items closed. **One genuine overturn**: `tsa_anchor` key 4 (`source`) is **removed from v1** — zero producers, zero consumers (the pipeline already refuses to copy bundle-recorded anchor metadata into the report), the only rendered `tstr` neither signed nor committed, and the only assigned key with no spec line; key 4 becomes plain reserved and its absence is asserted. Found `block_number` **under-determined** — S1's verified multi-tx path spans blocks, so two honest sealers emit different bytes — and gave it a total definition (minimum block over key 0). Found **D82 absent from the registry entirely**. Ruled the recorded `anchor_status` subset to be **no subset at all**: any narrowing would be a parse rule keyed on the field D79 forbids depending on. 30 substantive registry sections moved (predicted 30); 11 record errors listed in §17
- [x] **D9** GGM node-address representation ((level,index) vs bit-path) — co-frozen G8/F4 — **Resolved 2026-07-28: `(level, index)` pair of uints** — G8's implementation and the F4 draft converged independently; the index bits MSB-first ARE the root-to-node path, so it is the spec's own line-96 leaf rule generalized rather than a second convention, and canonical-CBOR uint encoding makes it canonical for free (a bit-path needs a pad-bit rule and its own rejection class). Wire shapes stay F4 Candidate A (`cover_entry`/`path_node` = `[level, index, bstr(32)]`); **co-frozen with the registry at Q14** (docs/decisions/D9-ggm-node-address.md)
- [x] **D10** Parser cap constants (bundle/manifest size, unit/file/anchor counts, list lengths, depth) (F11) — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28** (docs/decisions/D10-parser-caps.md): 19 frozen constants, one universal clamp rule `min(claimed_length, remaining_input)`, **18 new codes**, all enforced in verify stage 1 ahead of every AEAD/hash/signature; `u64` throughout so wasm32 and native cannot diverge. Cover/path caps are **derived, not guessed** (`2·⌈log₂ n⌉`; `size` is a uint so `d ≤ 64` bounds the honest max at 128 — the cap is 2×). Binding sizing case is the spec's n=10⁸ split text file with a mirror: 219 MiB, 14 % under the cap, the non-obvious term being padding. **Found that over-deep CBOR is unreachable through `SealProof::decode`** (fixed field types ⇒ `cbor-unexpected-type` at level 1), so F15's over-deep row is a direct-call row. Amended 2026-07-28 with three prose corrections from F11's implementation, incl. the ruling that the size cap precedes version dispatch (a >256 MiB v2 bundle reports `bundle-too-large`: cheapest rejection wins)
- [x] **D11** Autonomi address byte length pinned from ant-core source via S1 (F4/F5/F8) — **Resolved 2026-07-27**: **32 bytes** — `XorName = [u8; 32]`, BLAKE3-256 of chunk content (ant-protocol 2.3.0 src/chunk.rs:43; docs/research/S1-ant-core-api-survey.md)
- [x] **D12** Independent CBOR cross-check implementation (Python `cbor2` vs second Rust crate, dev-only) (F14/Q11) — **Resolved 2026-07-27**: Python `cbor2 ==6.1.3` dev-tool-only (independent lineage; boundary-value byte-identity proven; RFC 7049 ordering caveat recorded — moot for uint-only keys) (docs/decisions/D7-cbor-crate.md §D12)
- [x] **D13** `ed25519-dalek` 2.x vs 3.0.0 (P11/C11) — **Resolved 2026-07-27: `=3.0.0`** — strict-verify semantics probe-identical to 2.2.0 (byte-identical keys/sigs); single RustCrypto generation (signature 3 / sha2 0.11 / getrandom 0.4, shared with ml-dsa) vs 2.x demonstrably doubling the stack; ZIP-215 pubkey gap closed by C12 pre-validation (docs/decisions/D13-ed25519-dalek-pin.md)
- [x] **D14** Primary ML-DSA crate (ml-dsa vs fips204) + whether the Ed25519-only `sig_policy` fallback ships (C11) — **Resolved 2026-07-27: primary `ml-dsa =0.1.1`** (wasm32 probe PASSED with executed native↔wasm bit-match; complete FIPS-204 canonical rejection at `Signature::decode` — no C13 pre-validation needed); fallback `fips204 =0.4.6` pinned-unconsumed (byte-identical keygen/deterministic-sign proven — swap is a dep change, not a format event); **Ed25519-only fallback NOT shipped** (trigger did not fire) (docs/decisions/D14-mldsa-crate.md)
- [x] **D15** ML-DSA signing mode: hedged vs deterministic (affects golden-vector shape) (C16) — **Resolved 2026-07-27: deterministic (rnd = 0³²)**, implemented at C13. Portable C16 vectors, a byte-invisible fips204 fallback swap, one signing discipline with Ed25519, and no RNG in the signing path. Residual (recorded, threat model): hedged mode's fault/side-channel hardening is forgone — acceptable for a short-lived CLI process on the sealer's own machine with per-work keys. **Not format-encoded**, so a future version may switch without a format bump (docs/decisions/D15-mldsa-signing-mode.md)
- [x] **D16** Non-canonical-Ed25519-S rejection mechanism: crate parse-time vs explicit pre-validation (C12) — **Resolved 2026-07-27: explicit pre-validation layer in C12** — probe: dalek `Signature` parse is infallible on both 2.x/3.0.0 (S ≥ L checked only inside verify, opaque error), so parse-time rejection cannot produce the distinct `NonCanonicalSignature`; C12 pre-checks S < L and A-canonicality (ZIP-215 gap: decompress→recompress→compare) (C11 report §9)
- [x] **D17** Registered `sig_policy` algorithm-ID numeric values + reserved slots (C14/F4) — **FROZEN 2026-07-28 at Q14**. `0 = ed25519` (because the fallback policy is `[0]`), `1 = ml-dsa-65`, `2..=15` reserved and rejected. Ratified in code at C14/F5 in wave 3; the freeze needed no new test — `crypto_and_manifest_registries_agree` and `sig_alg_wire_mapping_matches_d17` already pin both directions over the full id range, and both mapping fns are wildcard-free. What F4 changed is the 9 `pending-D17` status markers, now `frozen-v1`. The 16-value universe freezes too, because D10's non-cap on `sig_policy`/`pubkeys`/`signatures` depends on it
- [ ] **D18** wasm-bindgen surface location: feature-gated in core vs thin wrapper crate (P14/R22; final by M3)
- [x] **D19** cargo-deny vs cargo-audit (or both) (P13) — **Resolved 2026-07-27: cargo-deny only** (`=0.19.8` CI pin; advisories/bans/sources active, licenses stubbed → D6/Q29 with BlueOak-1.0.0/GPL-3.0 notes; every ignore carries justification + review date); **amended same day (C11 finding): GHSA/osv.dev sweep mandatory** — RUSTSEC covers only 1 of 3 ml-dsa advisories (docs/decisions/D19-advisory-lane.md)
- [x] **D20** `--force-text` semantics on invalid UTF-8 (lossy U+FFFD total transform vs descriptor flag) (G2) — **Resolved 2026-07-27: deterministic lossy U+FFFD (Unicode §3.9 maximal subparts); text-mode canonicalization total; NO descriptor flag** — lossy ≡ strict on valid UTF-8, so `kind=Text` alone determines verifier recompute semantics; `--force-text` is a seal-time classification gate only; truncated-BOM → leading-U+FFFD corner KAT-pinned (docs/decisions/D20-force-text.md)
- [x] **D21** Canonicalization micro-semantics: lone-CR handling, single leading BOM, pipeline order (G2) — **Resolved 2026-07-27: pipeline decode → BOM strip → EOL → NFC frozen; strip ALL leading U+FEFF** (contiguous leading run — deliberate deviation from the strip-exactly-one proposal, forced by the normative idempotence property on multi-BOM inputs); one-pass CRLF→LF then lone CR→LF (CR CR LF → LF LF); canonical form never begins with U+FEFF, contains no CR, NFC (docs/decisions/D21-canonicalization-micro-semantics.md)
- [x] **D22** Blank-line definition for `--split` (whitespace-only lines; separator attachment) (G6) — **Resolved 2026-07-27: blank = content is solely ASCII space/tab (empty included), CommonMark-aligned**; separator run attaches to the **preceding** unit, leading run to the first, trailing run to the last. Rationale: a visually blank `"   \n"` treated as content would silently merge two paragraphs into one unit, so a user revealing "one paragraph" would disclose two. Exotic whitespace (U+00A0, U+3000, VT, FF) is deliberately non-blank — no Unicode table to freeze. **Seal-time CLI semantics, not verification law** (R checks only tiling), so changeable without a format bump; each seal's resulting ranges are permanent (docs/decisions/D22-split-blank-line-definition.md)
- [x] **D23** Raw-mirror placement in manifest order (proposal: appended after the file's normal units) — frozen forever (G5/G7) — **Resolved 2026-07-27: the mirror is its file's LAST unit**, at most one per file. Keeps id order ≡ range order for the tiling set, matches construction order (the mirror decision needs the finished canonical rendition), and ratifies the spec proposal + F4 draft. **R must not rely on it**: verifier exemption is by `kind`, so a hand-built manifest violating placement still verifies correctly (docs/decisions/D23-raw-mirror-placement.md)
- [x] **D24** `--no-fine-tree` × `--split` on one file: silent single-unit vs hard error (G5/G6/U13) — **Resolved 2026-07-27: hard CLI error** at plan validation (before the consent gate, before any payment), naming the file and both flags; binary files under `--split` are NOT an error (splitting never applies to them). Silent single-unit would permanently discard requested reveal granularity — irreversible and discovered only at first reveal. **Model-side it is unrepresentable, not checked**: G5's `SplitEligibleText` witness makes the contradiction unwritable (docs/decisions/D24-no-fine-tree-split-conflict.md)
- [x] **D25** Normalization crate + exact Unicode data version + multi-version retention architecture (G1/P7) — **Resolved 2026-07-27**: `unicode-normalization = "=0.1.25"` = Unicode **17.0.0** (verified from crate bytes; machine-asserted); descriptor `unicode-17.0.0`; append-only registry, future versions vendored side-by-side, shipped tables retained forever (docs/decisions/D25-unicode-normalization.md)
- [x] **D26** Perf/memory budget constants (C₁, corpus size, ceilings) (G18) — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28** (docs/decisions/D26-perf-memory-budgets.md): the framing is overturned — **`C₁` does not exist**. Both instrumented peaks are exact closed forms (`peak_seed_stack_len == d + 1`, `peak_frontier_len == bit_width(n)`), verified exhaustively for `n = 1..=4096` with zero mismatches, so a tolerance constant would license exactly the regression the test exists to catch; the entry's `<= C₁·⌈log₂ n⌉` form is also false at `n = 1`. Corpus sizes from measurement: CI runs the **dev** profile, where G18's suggested 256 MiB is **6.3 hours** (13.5 min in release). **Nothing here is format-permanent and G18 is not a Q14 dependency.** Also found G18's task text unimplementable twice over
- [x] **D27** `verify_bundle` error mode: fail-fast (tamper-authoritative) vs collect-all (rendering) (R1/R5/R7) — **Resolved 2026-07-27**: hybrid — fail-fast typed first-error is the sole normative mode (tamper matrix/Q7 bind to it); `VerifyFailures` is rendering-only, primary-first, first element ≡ the fail-fast error (docs/decisions/D27-verify-error-mode.md)
- [x] **D28** Full-reveal strictness: bundle revealing all non-mirror units MUST carry file_salt (+s_root if fine tree) or hard-fail (R4/R7) — **Resolved 2026-07-28: STRICT, hard-fail** — v1 has no "reveal every unit but withhold the whole-file openings" shape. Decisive argument: `canon_commit`/`raw_commit` have **no other verification path** (`fine_root`/`unit_commit` are per-unit at R2, `path_commit`/ranges at R3), so optionality would not weaken the check — it would **delete** it, leaving two signed, anchored fields a sealer may fill with anything. Also: strict→permissive is a legal future relaxation, permissive→strict is not (line 123), so strict is the reversible direction. Predicate is over `kind = Normal` units only, with an anti-vacuity `N(F) ≠ ∅` clause (a hand-built size-0 file tiles with zero units and would otherwise be vacuously "fully revealed"); `s_root` required iff `FineTree::Present`. Reuses R1's existing `FullRevealMaterialMissing` variant — no new codes. Residual (recorded): one `file_salt` covers both `raw_commit` and `canon_commit` (spec line 95), so a canonical-only full reveal is an offline oracle over the raw forms that canonicalize to the shown bytes — bounded, and far smaller than the guarantee permissiveness would surrender (docs/decisions/D28-full-reveal-strictness.md)
- [x] **D29** Deterministic verification-report byte format (the bit-match contract) (Q4/Q5/R1) — **FROZEN 2026-07-28 at Q14** as report **v1** (`REPORT_VERSION = 1`, bumped at R32). Byte-deterministic compact `serde_json`: declaration-order fields, Vec/BTreeMap only, no floats, lowercase hex, kebab-case wire names; `serde =1.0.229` / `serde_json =1.0.151` exact-pinned **and now asserted** (Q53 — the pin class was declared in policy and checked by nothing). Three enforcement layers, the load-bearing one being `FROZEN.sha256`'s post-freeze refusal to modify the 21-string report vector. **Q38 closed the gap that mattered**: until this wave the format was to freeze with *no* independent check at all. Report **v2 is already scheduled** for M2's anchor states — line 123 promises v1 stays verifiable, not that v1 is last (docs/decisions/D29-report-byte-format.md)
- [x] **D30** Stable machine-readable error-code contract across F/C/G/A/R (Q7/Q8) — **FROZEN 2026-07-28 at Q14**, and the freeze is what finally gave it a *mechanism*. Formalized at `docs/testing/error-code-contract.md` in wave 3, but until **Q52** the append-only rule was enforced by review discipline alone: all three existing layers check **distinctness** and **shape**, neither of which a rename violates, and **53 of 191 codes (28 %) could be renamed with a fully green suite** — measured, then validated live (renaming one produced exactly one failure across 706 tests). `testdata/error-codes/v1/CODES.txt` now snapshots the universe, compared every run, additions-only. Census also settled that **a prefix is not a partition of the enumerators**: `fine-root-` has 9 codes, not 8, because `fine-root-rebuild-mismatch` is minted by R — a second standing exception to §2, recorded in §7 because the code is frozen and cannot be renamed
- [x] **D31** Independent cross-check vehicles for the non-CBOR surfaces + permanence (one-shot artifact vs CI lane; is ml-dsa↔fips204+ACVP "independent" for ML-DSA? — the CBOR vehicle is D12's) (Q11) — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28** (docs/decisions/D31-cross-check-vehicles.md): one vehicle per surface, graded **T0** (external oracle) / **T1** (independent re-implementation) / **T2** (two crates in one ecosystem). The grading dissolves the register's hard question — `ml-dsa`↔`fips204` is T2, so it was never independence but D14's fallback check wearing the wrong label. **ML-DSA-65 = NIST ACVP (T0)**, which makes it the *best*-covered surface rather than the worst; Q11's Notes had it backwards. `cbor2` **decodes only**; our own §4.2.1 encoder is the encoding authority, retiring D12's ordering caveat. Permanence: **both** — a dated one-shot report gating Q14 plus a permanent unconditional CI lane. Found the report byte format would have frozen with no cross-check at all (→ Q38). **Executed at Q11: 0 discrepancies across 13 rows**
- [x] **D74** Extraneous `s_root` on a `FineTree::Absent` full reveal: reject vs silently ignore (R4/F8) — surfaced 2026-07-28 by D28's planning pass as the one **forced** second choice (R4's typed classifier has nowhere to put it). **Recommended: reject**, following C14's present-set-==-required-set precedent; proposed `FullRevealSRootWithoutFineTree { file_id }` → `full-reveal-s-root-without-fine-tree` (single code — a `FileSalt` arm would be unreachable). Resolve with R4 — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28** (docs/decisions/D74-extraneous-full-reveal-s-root.md): **reject**, ratified — and the window is recorded as *already closed*, since the code has landed with a row behind it, so reversing to "ignore" would delete a code (contract §3) in the permissive direction (line 123). Three corrections to the record, incl. that row 3 is reachable **only** by stripping the whole §7.14 entry — "simplifying" it to a missing-key mutation would silently re-bind it from R's code to F8's `bundle-missing-key`
- [x] **D75** Full-reveal cover shape: does a full reveal ship per-unit covers **and** `s_root` (F8/F9) — surfaced 2026-07-28 (D28). D28 holds either way; this decides whether R4's `s_root` fine-root rebuild is load-bearing or defence-in-depth. Resolve with F8 — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28** (docs/decisions/D75-full-reveal-cover-shape.md): **BOTH** — a full reveal ships per-unit covers *and* `s_root`. Outcome confirmed, **consequences overturned twice**: the "additionally proves no extra leaves exist" claim is vacuous (both routes take `n` from the signed `size`), and the `s_root` rebuild is not merely defence-in-depth — `check_fine_root_rebuild` is the **sole** consumer of the disclosed `s_root` anywhere, so deleting it would recreate exactly the unchecked bundle-side material D74 mints a permanent code to reject. That is what makes D74 and D75 one rule rather than two opinions
- [x] **D76** Split `file_salt` into separate raw/canonical salts (C2/C6) — surfaced 2026-07-28 (D28's residual-risk analysis: one salt covers both `raw_commit` and `canon_commit`, so a canonical-only full reveal is an offline oracle over the raw forms that canonicalize to the shown bytes). **RESOLVED 2026-07-28: NO for v1** (docs/decisions/D76-file-salt-split.md) — outcome confirmed, **its recorded reason overturned**: the "new HKDF label" cost is near-nil pre-Q14, because C2's info encoding is injective *by construction* precisely so labels can be added safely. The arguments that hold: (1) both salts' presence rules would read the manifest, dropping registry §7.14 key 1 from tier [P] to [R] under D78 and breaking the fact D28 rule 3 and D74's single-code argument rest on; (2) the permanent reveal-shape code surface roughly doubles, 5 → 8–9; (3) **the split does not eliminate the leak** — the mirror's `true_length` publishes raw size in plaintext and its existence asserts raw ≠ canonical, so the oracle only resolves same-length ambiguity. Exposure is exactly one of four full-reveal cases and leaks EOL/BOM/normalization metadata, never content
- [x] **D77** Zero-non-mirror-unit file: structural rejection vs tolerated (R3/F5) — surfaced 2026-07-28 (D28). `verify::structural::tile` accepts a hand-built size-0 file with zero units (`cursor == size` at 0); **RESOLVED 2026-07-28: REJECT at F5** (docs/decisions/D77-zero-unit-file.md), code `manifest-empty-normal-units` via a fifth `ContainerField::NormalUnits` variant — **implemented same day**. The substance was corrected in the process: the shape this entry and D28 describe (size-0 file, zero units) is **already closed** by F5's `manifest-empty-units` and cannot reach R3 from a decoded manifest. The hole that actually survives F5/R3/R4 is the **mirror-only file** (`N(F) = ∅` with a non-empty unit table), which no record named — it permanently un-opens `canon_commit`/`raw_commit`, reinstating per-file exactly what D28 rationale 1 exists to prevent. So it was a hole, not hardening
- [x] **D78** May bundle schema validation open the embedded manifest? (F8/F9, D8 §13 item 8) — surfaced 2026-07-28 by the D8 bundle pass; **Ratified same day: NO.** At schema time the bundle treats `manifest` as an opaque `bstr`; the manifest layer decodes it and owns the `manifest-`/`cbor-` codes. Rationale: F3 already established outer/inner layered decode with distinct error classes and F6 keeps the body **as received** with no re-encode path — if bundle validation opened the manifest, manifest-level failures would surface inside the bundle's error family, and D30 makes code families **permanent**. This is not layering taste: it fixes which error family cross-side rejections land in, and F8's error enum needs it before it is written (docs/decisions/D78-bundle-schema-manifest-opacity.md — record written wave 7, Q57)
- [x] **D79** OTS upgrade group (`block_height`/`block_header`/`fetch_date`): free-standing all-or-nothing vs conditioned on `status == attested` (F8, A-domain semantics) — surfaced 2026-07-28 (D8 §13 item 9); **Ratified same day: FREE-STANDING.** The group is structural — all three present or all three absent — and the **parse rule never depends on `status`**, because `status` is sealer-written and the design says a verifier must not trust it; conditioning parse on it would let a sealer choose which shapes decode. Same derived-not-declared principle as D28. **Scope: the parse rule only** — what the status values mean and how the verifier derives the real one stays A-domain at M2 (docs/decisions/D79-ots-upgrade-group-presence.md — record written wave 7, Q57)
- [x] **D80** Must a revealed unit's owning file appear in `touched_files`? (F8/R4, D8 §13 item 10) — surfaced 2026-07-28. **Resolved same day at F8: REQUIRED**, on spec evidence — line 95 says `path_salt` ships whenever any reveal **touches** the file (so the recipient can verify the path), and line 121 defines only two rendering states (revealed content, or unrevealed files with the path withheld); the permissive reading needs a third state the spec never defines. The privacy objection that framed this entry **overstated the stake**: the plaintext manifest already exposes file identity, size and unit boundaries by design (line 95), so path-hiding would have hidden only the *filename*. Tier `[R]` — it needs the manifest, so D78 keeps the check out of F8; recommended code `revealed-unit-file-not-touched`, spelling and enforcement owned by R4/R5 (docs/decisions/D80-revealed-unit-touched-file.md)
- [x] **D81** `unit-decrypt-failed` has no cause discriminator, so two spec-mandated M0 tamper rows are indistinguishable (R8/Q8) — surfaced 2026-07-28 by Q8's completeness registry applying distinctness to **pending** rows, i.e. before either row exists. *Flipped ciphertext byte* and *swapped unit* both land on `unit-decrypt-failed` because `VerifyError::UnitDecryptFailed` carries no cause, so R8 as specified **cannot have both rows**. Options: (a) mint a cause discriminator — this **changes the frozen code set**, so it must happen before Q14; (b) keep one as a row and demote the other to an R10 property. **RESOLVED 2026-07-28: option (b), NO discriminator minted** (docs/decisions/D81-unit-decrypt-cause.md) — **overturning this entry's own recommendation**. The premise was backwards: the AEAD is not the binding mechanism for either mutation (line 103 puts all content/identity binding in the salted commitments) and both are independently caught at stage 4; the code names the stage that fired, not the attacker's intent. Two real candidate discriminators were evaluated and rejected with costs stated — the manifest's BLAKE3 chunk address (makes an M3 storage-linkage computation verdict-bearing at M0, requires interpreting a deliberately-opaque `ContentAddress` in the WASM verdict path, and is evaded by swap-then-flip) and speculative re-decryption (O(n²) on hostile input, blind to non-revealed donors, and makes the reported code a function of *unrelated* bundle contents). R stays at 84 codes. Outcome: one row, three recorded non-rows — R8 found a **third** collision of the same shape (wrong `unit_salt`) → R33. It also forced Q8's registry to gain a third case state, without which Q14's gate would have stayed red forever. Two related hazards are already recorded in `testdata/tamper/MATRIX.json` and need no decision — a G19/R8 pair on `fine-root-binding-failed` and a G19/R7 pair on `fine-root-over-broad-cover` each yield **one** row, because R's wrapper arms surface the inner code unchanged (error-code contract §2)
- [x] **D82** Is a `touched_files` entry for a file with **no** revealed unit legitimate? (R5/R7) — surfaced 2026-07-28 at R5. D80 settled only the converse (a revealed unit's file **must** be listed). Today the disclosed-path-without-reveal direction is **allowed** and renders as a placeholder; rejecting it would mint a permanent code for a rule no decision has taken, so R5 deliberately did not. Note it is a real disclosure shape — proving *a file by this path was part of the work* without revealing any of its content — so the permissive reading may well be correct. **RESOLVED 2026-07-28: REJECT** (docs/decisions/D82-touched-file-without-reveal.md), minting `touched-file-without-revealed-unit` / `VerifyError::TouchedFileWithoutRevealedUnit { file_id }` (R 84 → 85 codes, 26 → 27 variants), firing at R5 coherence group 1b — additive to the frozen order, asserted. **Implemented same day.** Decisive evidence: D80 argued from an *absent* rendering state while line 121 states the opposite outright (unrevealed files render as committed placeholders), and line 95 makes it universal. It is also the D74 shape in a second section — `path_salt` is a per-work constant, so a relay holding any bundle of the work holds a genuine `{path, path_salt}` pair and can splice it into a bundle whose sealer withheld that file, passing R3's `path_commit` check because it is authentic
- [x] **D83** A leaf-level GGM cover seed's upper 16 bytes are inert (G20/G23) — surfaced 2026-07-28 by G20's proof-mutation property at `PROPTEST_CASES=1024`, counterexample committed. A cover node at `level == d` covers exactly one leaf, so the verifier descends zero levels and reads `salt_i = seed[..16]`: **bytes 16..32 of that node's disclosed 32-byte seed are never examined**, and a bundle may carry anything there and still verify. Not a soundness or confidentiality break — it is a canonical-form problem: two byte-distinct `.sealproof` files carry the same proof and both verify, a `bundle-cover-seed-bitflip` row could never be landed as written, and 16 bytes per node are wasted. Leaf-level nodes appear in most partial reveals, including G11's first normative KAT. Options: (A) accept + document in registry §5, (B) require a fixed canonical tail, (C) encode a `level == d` cover payload as **16 bytes**, keeping 32 for `level < d`. **Format event either way — must be settled before Q14.** Recommendation: C, fallback A (docs/decisions/D83-leaf-cover-seed-tail-malleability.md). Owned by **G23** — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28: option B, the canonical zero tail** (docs/decisions/D83-leaf-cover-seed-tail-malleability.md) — **overturning the recommendation of C**. A `level == d` payload is `salt_i ‖ 0x00·16`: still `bstr(32)`, still tier [P] on length, with the zero tail a tier-[R] value rule; zero decoder changes, zero tier changes, one code minted. C loses on a fact no record had checked — MVP-SPEC.md lines **96 and 121** both fix "every GGM covering seed = 32 B" outright, so C is a **spec amendment**, not a registry change — and it would drop an exact byte length (§0's archetypal [P] check) to [R] for two fields, the same argument that decided D76, for a saving measured at **<= 0.95 %**. Scope widened beyond what the record named: the rule also binds `full_reveal.s_root` at `n == 1`. **Implemented same day at G23**
- [x] **D84** Are A's M2 `.ots`/DER structural limits format-permanent? (A5/A11/Q14) — surfaced 2026-07-28 by D10's analysis. A5 already promises hard caps (max response size, cert count/size, signed-attribute count, nesting depth) and A11 "strict limits", but both land at **M2, after Q14 closes** — yet those limits decide accept/reject on a **v1-frozen** bundle, which is the same argument that pulled D10's opaque-artifact byte caps into M0. **Recommendation: scope them explicitly as post-freeze and document artifact-internal limits as a named exception to spec line 123** — the alternative is guessing DER limits at M0 with no recorded real token to check against. Blocks A5, A11 and Q14's freeze *scope*. (This is the question D10's author proposed as "D83"; that number went to G20's finding, which landed a doc the same day.) — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28** (docs/decisions/D84-anchor-artifact-limits-permanence.md): the limits are **NOT v1 format surface** and do not freeze at Q14 — scoping confirmed, **mechanism overturned**: there is *no* named exception to line 123, because line 123 is never at risk (M0/M1 render every anchor `absent`, pinned on disk by R10, so no released verdict depends on artifact internals). Carving a permanent exception into a normative stability rule to buy nothing is a bad trade. What **does** freeze, and had to be decided now, is structural: rules **F1–F4**. Also ruled that A5/A11's promised caps **are** the existing D10 constants, removing four from the open set. **Recorded at A27**
- [x] **D85** `unit_commit` mismatch cause discriminator (R33/Q8) — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28: no code minted** (docs/decisions/D85-unit-commit-cause.md), the non-row ratified and the rule generalised to **every** salted-commitment opening in R's pipeline. The asymmetry R33 named is real — the salt is bundle-side, the commit manifest-side, the manifest signed — but it yields no attribution and the split would be **unsound**, not merely unhelpful: the verifier never holds `W`, so anyone holding the bundle can re-encrypt arbitrary plaintext under the supplied `k_u` and pass stage 1 (line 103). "Signature invalid" names no field either, since one signature covers the whole body, and neither half separates a third-party edit from an adversarial sealer — which *is* the threat model. A cause belongs in **rendering** (D27's `VerifyFailures`), never in the code set. R stays put — D85 minted nothing. (2026-07-31 correction: the entry's original closing "85 codes / 27 variants" was stale at writing — G23/D83's `fine-root-leaf-seed-tail-not-zero` had landed hours earlier the same day, so the universe at the freeze is **86 codes / 28 variants**, 27 of them exemplar-represented; `verify/error.rs` `DISTINCT_CODES = 86` is the source of truth.)
- [x] **D86** Does the decode **layer** reach the user-facing verdict? (F26/R9/Q7) — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28: it never enters the report**, and the two `layer()` accessors harmonise **upward** — total on both types, `Option` dropped, manifest side derived from a new `ManifestError::map()` (docs/decisions/D86-decode-layer-in-verdict.md). **Overturned F26's own proposal** to harmonise downward. D27 §4 makes the report exist only for a bundle that *passed*, and `layer` occurs **zero** times across all 21 pinned report byte strings, so F26's "expensive after Q14" premise was unreachable. The real defect was elsewhere: `body-unknown-key` and `envelope-unknown-key` are **one observable**, a live counterexample to the error-code contract's own §2 claim (→ F27). **Implemented same day at F26**
- [x] **D87** Bit-match vector carriage before v2 doubles the tree (F21/Q5/Q6/R28) — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28: keep `include_bytes!`**, with a 2 MiB per-version ceiling enforced by a `build.rs` panic rather than a comment (docs/decisions/D87-bitmatch-vector-carriage.md). **Overturned F21's framing by measurement**: carriage is **0.73 %** of the lane and 2.32 % of the artifact, while *executing* the vectors is **90.1 %**. The alternatives optimise the 1 % and each destroys a structural property — the module's zero imports, the crate's zero unsafe, and decisively "both sides execute identical bytes" as a **link-time fact** rather than a claim about the JS runner's plumbing (→ F28, F29). **Implemented same day at F21**
- [x] **D88** Disposition of the un-zeroized HKDF/HMAC key state (C22/C21 R1) — **RESOLVED 2026-07-28: option (d), which C22 did not list — enable `sha2`'s non-default `zeroize` feature** (docs/decisions/D88-hkdf-hmac-zeroization.md). **C22's and the audit's shared premise "no feature fixes it" is FALSE**: `digest`'s `buffer_fixed!` `ZeroizeOnDrop` arm emits a *marker impl with no `Drop`*, so the trait was never the mechanism — the wipe is ordinary drop glue reaching `Sha256VarCore::drop`, gated on a crate neither C21 nor C22 examined. Measured: 114 of 144 bytes of `Hkdf<Sha256>` non-zero, and **`W` sitting verbatim** in the extract context's block buffer, which R1 had recorded as merely "`W`-equivalent". Option (b) would not compile; (c) is strictly weaker alone. Also closed an unrecorded gap — every `ggm::child_seed` left its **parent seed fully recoverable** in a dropped hasher. **Implemented same day at C22/C23/C24**; four prose errors amended back into the record, one of which would have aborted a correct implementation

### Due M1
- [x] **D32** Blob↔address model (data-map style vs per-chunk) per ant-core's real storage model (S2/S4/S6; informed by S1) — 2026-07-27 inputs captured in the S1 memo (recommended: blob = one chunk, 32-B BLAKE3 address; `get_data` ↦ `chunk_get`) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D32-blob-address-model.md): **chunk-level** — every blob is exactly one Autonomi chunk, address = BLAKE3-256(ciphertext), `get_data` ↦ `chunk_get`, and **no data-map path in v1**: the S1 lean is confirmed but its open oversize door is closed — a blob whose ciphertext exceeds `MAX_CHUNK_SIZE` (4 MiB; max unit plaintext 4 194 047 B) is a **seal-time plan-validation hard error** before consent, anchors, and quote, a duty that is not optional because upstream quotes and pays for unstorable chunks without complaint. Spec deviation recorded (lines 34/51/110/119 "via self_encryption" → BLAKE3-256); the large-blob path is parked to v1.1
- [x] **D33** Block-number acquisition locus: antseal-net pay() vs antseal-anchor RPC client (S7/A17) — 2026-07-27 inputs captured: no upstream payment API returns a block number; own `eth_getTransactionReceipt` mandatory (S1 memo) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D33-block-number-locus.md): **`antseal-net::pay()` — the register's anchor-side framing overturned**: the payment RPC is not anchor HTTP. In the primary external-signer flow the block number is a field of the very tx receipt `pay()` must await anyway; the native-fallback and crash-backfill paths read `eth_getTransactionReceipt` over the payment RPC S5's own `NetworkConfig` already defines (`ant_protocol` `http_provider` re-export — no dependency beyond the alloy `Provider` trait pay's submission machinery needs). `antseal-anchor` gets **zero M1 work**; A17 stays M2/advisory and consumes the S7 receipt, never produces it. Spec line 52 flagged for amendment (capture → confirmation)
- [x] **D34** Seal-pipeline crate placement (CLI lib target vs orchestration module; must be library-drivable) (S12/S10) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D34-seal-pipeline-placement.md): **`antseal-cli` gains a `[lib]` target** — the register's lean confirmed, but on grounds it never stated (it recorded no rationale at all): pipeline-in-core is structurally impossible (`CostQuote`/`PaymentReceipt` are churn surface and core never depends on net), pipeline-in-net violates the storage crate's spec-written charter (anchor/consent/journal surface excluded), and a dedicated orchestration crate breaks the cargo publishability chain while D72 is still open. The orchestration layer (S12 pipeline, S10 journal state machine, S11 resume, S14 restore — and M3's R16 reveal flow, forced out of core by the same dependency direction) is `antseal_cli` library code over injected interfaces (StorageBackend, the A1 gate, lib-defined ConsentHook/SealJournal); `main.rs` stays thin. Forced sub-finding: `MockBackend` must be consumable across a crate boundary under EVERY option → S3 feature-export note
- [x] **D35** self_encryption in core: direct pinned dep vs vendored address-derivation subset (S4/P15) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D35-self-encryption-dependency-mode.md): **option (d) — the register's option space overturned: NO antseal crate depends on `self_encryption` at all.** Under D32 (blob = one chunk) S4's `compute_storage_address` is `blake3` alone (new exact-pinned workspace dep, default-features off); `antseal-net` takes addresses from ant-core's own returns and cross-checks them against S4. P15 re-scoped from pin to **CI prohibition** (`self_encryption` only ever transitive under ant-core, linked only into net/cli binaries); D6's CRITICAL GPL flag discharged by deletion — no GPL code approaches core or the WASM page. Decisive evidence: 0.36.0 carries mandatory tokio/rayon/tempfile/rand, so it is wasm32-dead regardless of license. Revisit triggers recorded (P19 relicense watch; the v1.1 large-blob decision)
- [x] **D36** Pre-pay resume re-quote/re-consent policy on cost change (proposed: re-consent) (S11/U17) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D36-prepay-resume-requote-reconsent.md): **always re-quote, consent per-invocation — S11's "re-consent if cost changed" conditional overturned; U17's unconditional wording ratified** (the task-text split arbitrated). A journaled quote is never paid: nothing in the pay path checks age before moving tokens, and storers enforce ~24 h `QUOTE_MAX_AGE_SECS` — so re-quoting is mandatory anyway, and "unchanged" is not even well-defined below the totals. One invariant falls out: **consent is a single-use spend authorization consumed by the `pay()` it immediately precedes, in the same process invocation** — subsuming D37's proofs-expired re-consent and the no-consent-post-receipt rule as corollaries. Decline → `incomplete`, never abandon; "changed" is display-only (prior consented totals + drift flag from a new S10 consent record `{total_ant_atto, gas_estimate, consent_time, channel}`); `--yes` affirms the fresh quote; D51 governs non-interactive uniformly
- [x] **D37** Multi-tx payment handling (per-tx chunk cap; plural tx hashes) (S6/S7) — 2026-07-27 inputs captured: 256 transfers/tx, 64-chunk waves, 256 merkle leaves; `PaymentReceipt` needs `Vec<TxHash>` (S1 memo) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D37-multi-tx-payment.md): caps confirmed against source (the 64-chunk waves are upstream's driver shape, not a cap we hit), but the register's `Vec<TxHash>` conclusion is **overturned as materially insufficient** — `finalize_batch_payment` consumes a `HashMap<QuoteHash, TxHash>` and errors on any gap, so the receipt's core is the quote→tx **map** plus per-blob `{address, peer quotes, proof_bytes}` plus per-tx block-number slots; a bare hash list can neither rebuild proofs nor resume finalize. `pay()` emits ⌈blobs/256⌉ **sequential** txs journaled per sub-batch as each lands; merkle payment mode **excluded** from the paid path (its proofs carry no tx hashes — the spec's "one Merkle-batch EVM tx" is a recorded deviation: common case, not contract); `Client::batch_pay` **forbidden** in the native fallback (its error path discards the partial paid map — drive `Wallet::pay_for_quotes` directly). **New finding: node-side proof validity ≈ 24 h (`QUOTE_MAX_AGE_SECS`) time-boxes every post-pay resume** → new proofs-expired state with re-consent before any re-payment (rule supplied by D36)
- [x] **D38** Test-ANT acquisition mechanism on Arbitrum Sepolia 421614 (P17) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D38-sepolia-test-ant-acquisition.md): an ordinary **ERC-20 `transfer` from an existing holder — nothing else exists**: the deployed test token (0x4bc1aCE0E66170375462cB4E6Af42Ad4D5EC689C; payment-vault proxy 0xd742E8CFEf27A9a884F3EFfA239Ee2F39c276522, evmlib-0.9.0 src/lib.rs:58-76) has no mint function, upstream operates no faucet, and no bridge is involved. Primary route is maintainer-owned per the external-actions rule (community transfer request + account-gated gas faucets). Contingency, scripted and dormant: self-deploy both verified contract artifacts on 421614 via upstream's Custom-network machinery — a recorded deviation that never substitutes for the canonical-contract M4 gate
- [x] **D39** `init` interaction model: wizard vs flags (U1/U11) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D39-init-interaction-model.md): **the dichotomy is overturned as a false choice** — `init` is a TTY wizard whose **every question has a flag/fd equivalent** (a pure wizard violates three committed M1 Accept criteria; pure flags cannot carry the passphrase). The v1 `init` flag set is enumerated as a deliberate U1 surface amendment: `--wallet <generate|import>` (default `generate`), `--wallet-key-fd <n>` (D41 mechanism, must differ from `--passphrase-fd`), the global `--passphrase-fd <n>`, one D40 KDF selector slot and one `--wrap` selector slot (default declined) — nothing else. Existing-vault refusal is absolute: no `--force`, the error names the path and says move/remove it manually
- [x] **D40** KDF selection mechanism + low-RAM (cannot allocate 256 MiB) behavior (U6/U11) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D40-vault-kdf-selection.md): **Argon2id (m = 262144 KiB, t = 3, p = 1) sole default; scrypt (N = 2²⁰, r = 8, p = 1) only as an explicit init-time selection; low-RAM = typed hard error at create AND unlock — U6's fallback-prompt note overturned**: the fallback it imagines does not exist, because scrypt at its spec floor needs 1 GiB, four times Argon2id's 256 MiB. Adds **pre-auth KDF-header caps** (D10 precedent — a substituted header demanding 1 TiB is a resource bomb *before* the AEAD authenticates; the same caps serve unlock AND D47 import). Crates: scrypt `=0.12.0` (resolves onto the already-wiped sha2 =0.11.0 stack); argon2 0.5.3 **rejected** (a second un-wiped blake2/digest-0.10 stack) → recommend `=0.6.0-rc.8` plus a D88-style blake2 residue probe at the pin PR. Spec line 143 completion flagged (it names no scrypt r/p)
- [x] **D41** Non-interactive passphrase supply channel (env var vs fd vs keystore) (U7/U13) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D41-noninteractive-passphrase-channel.md): a global **`--passphrase-fd <n>`** (stdin is the degenerate case `--passphrase-fd 0`). The env-var channel is **rejected on a measured mechanism**: on Linux `/proc/PID/environ` serves the execve-time snapshot, which no userland `unsetenv` or zeroize can wipe — an env passphrase contradicts spec line 143's zeroization mandate for the whole process lifetime. The OS keystore is not a scripting channel (stays U8/D50). Byte semantics frozen: read-to-EOF with a 1 KiB cap, strip exactly one trailing LF/CRLF, reject empty and interior NUL/LF, prompt parity (fd bytes ≡ what the prompt would have produced); the fd path skips the create-time confirmation prompt
- [x] **D42** Vault encryption-boundary partition (config/anchor artifacts inside vs beside the AEAD; hook-without-unlock consequence) (U5; affects U24 at M2) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D42-vault-encryption-boundary.md): lean confirmed and sharpened — beside the AEAD sits **exactly** `config.toml` + the vault header + the lockfile; **everything else lives inside, including all anchor artifacts** (`.ots`, TSA tokens, fetch dates). The U24 consequence becomes a positive rule: the opportunistic-upgrade hook runs **iff the host command already holds an unlocked vault handle** — on a locked or absent vault it is a silent no-op, never a prompt, and it never creates `~/.antseal`. Rider: every AEAD in the vault binds the KDF header + record identity as AAD (splice resistance); whole-vault rollback protection is recorded out of scope
- [x] **D43** Staged-ciphertext retention after successful finalize (local cache vs prune) (U9/U27/U28) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D43-staged-ciphertext-retention.md): **RETAIN** — on finalize success the staged bytes reclassify journal → cache in place (same bytes, state tag only), stay inside the AEAD boundary, and are **excluded from `vault export`**. The journal/cache split is the load-bearing line: journal bytes (work incomplete) are resume-critical, never prunable, always exported; cache bytes (work complete) are integrity-rechecked (S4 recompute) on every use with silent `get_data` refetch fallback, and their loss is never an error. No prune surface at MVP (`vault gc` = v1.1 — R16 needs whole-file ciphertexts for boundary Merkle paths, so pruning would force full-file fetches). The proposed S14 amendment (network-first restore with verified-cache fallback) **ratified at integration 2026-08-01**
- [x] **D44** Wallet import formats (raw hex only vs +mnemonic) (U11) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D44-wallet-import-formats.md): **raw hex private key only in v1** (64 hex digits, optional `0x`, case-insensitive, k256 scalar-validated), with the accepted set defined as **"whatever the pinned evmlib/alloy parse accepts"** so import validation and the payment path cannot drift (evmlib wallet.rs:72-75 / alloy private_key.rs:224-230 — validation ≡ payment path). Mnemonic rejected for v1: the pinned upstream stack does not compile it in, it pulls two new payment-path crates (coins-bip32/39) into the exact-pin class, and derivation-path ambiguity is a silent wrong-wallet failure mode. Revisit trigger recorded
- [x] **D45** Resume detection keying + consent-on-resume rule (U17) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D45-resume-detection-consent.md): detection is **automatic, keyed on recorded invocation identity** (network + exact ordered lexically-absolutized input-path list + seal-shaping flag set): an exact match auto-resumes; **any overlap that is not an exact match, or a flag mismatch, is a hard error before consent/payment** (D24 pattern — two new resume-safety error classes); disjoint proceeds as a fresh seal with a notice. Interactive selection and a `--resume`/work-id flag rejected (not in the canonical surface; the crash-re-run path must be zero-interaction-safe). Consent: pre-pay resume re-fires the **full U14 gate over a fresh quote** (register lean confirmed — agrees with D36, whose cost-comparison rule this record deliberately defers to); post-receipt resume never re-consents and needs no `--yes`
- [x] **D46** Directory arguments to `seal`: recurse vs files-only error (U13) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D46-seal-directory-arguments.md): **files only** — directory, non-regular-file, and lexical-duplicate arguments hard-error at plan validation, before consent and payment (D24 pattern), identically under `--dry-run`; shell globbing is the recursion tool. Rejected not because recursion is unwanted but because every one of its sub-rules (iteration order, hidden files, symlinks, non-regular files — POSIX `readdir` order is unspecified and would reach the manifest file-table order) would be frozen per seal into a permanent paid artifact, with no human checkpoint left under `--yes`; error-now → recursion-later is the additive, reversible direction. G14/G5 may consequently assume regular files
- [x] **D47** `vault export` format (verbatim archive vs re-encrypted single file) (U12) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D47-vault-export-format.md): **re-encrypted single file** — the Q2-reserved magic `ANTSEAL VAULT EXPORT` + a versioned deterministic-CBOR header (own KDF block, fresh salt, AAD = magic‖header) + **one AEAD over the full serialized logical vault state** (config INCLUDED, cache EXCLUDED per D43); same vault passphrase, proven by the unlock the export performs; keyfile factor preserved; mandatory post-write self-verify; import validates everything in memory under D40's pre-auth caps before an atomic install. The register carried no lean; decided on two properties the verbatim archive cannot have — **whole-backup authentication** (per-record AEADs cannot detect *absence*: a backup missing works imports "successfully") and a deliberate interchange format that does not freeze U5's on-disk layout
- [x] **D48** `restore` default output dir + overwrite policy (U20) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D48-restore-output-policy.md): default output = the work-scoped fresh `./antseal-restore-<work-id>/`; recorded paths re-rooted under it by a **defensive lexical rule** (absolute prefix stripped, `..` rejected, intra-work collisions abort pre-write); overwrite policy **per-file and byte-aware** — an existing identical file counts as already-restored success (idempotent re-run), an existing different file is a per-file refused-overwrite error and is never touched, and files failing verification are never written (S14). Temp+rename writes; restore never prompts; processing continues past per-file errors and the exit code reports the most severe class present. In-place restore stays possible explicitly (`-o`), never by default
- [x] **D49** `--dry-run` network semantics: real quote_batch (proposed) vs offline estimate (U16) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D49-dry-run-network-semantics.md): **real `quote_batch`**, confirming the register's lean and S12's Accept, with four sharpenings: dry-run is the real pipeline **truncated after quoting** (a prefix, not a parallel mode); a live **S12↔U16 contradiction found and resolved** (S12:160's journal-before-backend invariant vs U16:201's zero-vault-mutation — the invariant is scoped to the **paid** path, dry-run runs journal-disabled with its own S16 matrix row); dry-run runs the S8 preflight and exits with the **real** insufficient-ANT/-gas codes, making it a scriptable preflight gate; and the quoted cost is labeled **indicative** — the fresh-nonce argument proving a dry-run leaks nothing linkable to a later real seal also means the real seal re-quotes different addresses from different peers
- [x] **D50** OS-keystore platform scope for the W wrap (U8) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D50-os-keystore-scope.md): **the framing is overturned — NO keystore platforms at M1**; the keyfile wrap ships alone, with zero new dependencies. The register's own question ("which platforms?") is unanswerable before D72 (release target set, due M4) without pre-committing D72, so the correct M1 answer is "no platforms yet, and the header makes later ones additive": U8's AAD-bound **wrap-mode registry** (0 = none, 1 = keyfile, 2 = os-keystore RESERVED, with a distinct "wrap mode not supported" error). Evidence against shipping now: keyring 4.x is churn-active; linux-keyutils is reboot-volatile (would brick the vault); machine-bound factors break D47's clean-machine drill. Platform scope + implementation = a recorded post-D72 follow-up (see the D72 row)
- [x] **D51** `--json` × interactivity contract (hard-abort without --yes: proposed yes) (U3/U14) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D51-json-interactivity-contract.md): hard-abort **confirmed and widened** — machine mode (`--json` ∨ non-TTY stdin ∨ stdin consumed by `--passphrase-fd 0`) never prompts for **anything**: consent, passphrase, wizard questions, or destructive confirms, each prompt class carrying a declared non-interactive channel or a deliberate absence of one (`vault import` over an existing vault has **no bypass flag by design** — scripted overwrite-import unsupported in v1). The consent report always renders (stderr under `--json`); declined ≡ unobtainable = **one** consent-not-obtained exit class, distinct from passphrase-unavailable and usage; interactivity gated on stdin `isatty`, `/dev/tty` rejected; post-receipt resume is not consent-bearing and proceeds without `--yes`. U3 gains the prompt-class registry + an abort-not-hang harness over every command × machine-mode flag
- [x] **D52** Devnet E2E venue: CI job vs self-hosted vs required local gate (Q15) — **Resolved 2026-08-01** (docs/decisions/D52-devnet-e2e-venue.md): **register lean overturned** — a **required LOCAL gate** (`scripts/e2e-devnet.sh`, mandatory before merging storage-touching changes, scripted evidence per the Q14 pattern) plus a **scheduled, non-required GitHub-hosted job** at reduced node count on the fuzz-nightly pattern, landing only if its measured runtime fits the minutes budget. A per-PR required CI job is rejected: branch protection 403s on Free-private (a 20th required context is unenforceable), the hosted runner is the same 2-core class against a 688-package graph vs the 10 GiB cache cap, and S18's SIGKILL matrix is flake-prone there; self-hosted rejected (the only candidate host is the 2-core dev machine itself — a standing runner daemon adds surface and contention for zero capability the local gate lacks). Q15's "cached node binaries" premise is obsolete — nodes are in-process (P16). Promote-to-required trigger recorded: plan upgrade or the Q65 public flip, after ≥20 clean scheduled runs with warm runtime ≤15 min. Scheduled-lane triage convention: a red scheduled run → a wave-bookkeeping note; two consecutive reds block storage-wave starts
- [x] **D89** Wallet primitives below the `ant-backend` gate: does a default build have wallet/balance capability, or is `init` feature-gated too? (U37/U11/U14) — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D89-wallet-primitives-below-the-gate.md): the light half moves **into the default graph** over exact-pinned `k256 =0.13.4` (**without** `ecdsa`) + `sha3 =0.11.0` — measured **120 → 129** packages default, **zero** added to the 447/475 heavy graphs, **zero** new `Cargo.lock` entries; `BalanceReport`/`PreflightReport` move at zero cost and `balances()` joins the `StorageBackend` trait. **Four framings overturned, three of them the brief's own**: dep-graph rule 1 asserts the *absence of five names*, not the package count, so **no lane assertion changes**; the real D44 gate crate is `alloy-signer-local` at **+71**, six times the k256 route; a default build already cannot seal (settled at S12/S22/U36), so nothing about "what a default build is" moves; and option (a) fails on **coverage, not taste** — required CI is default-features-only and S22's tier-2 trigger does not name `init`'s files, so four of U11's six Accept rows would be compiled by no gate at all (cfg-inactive code is not even type-checked; the concrete instance of open S30). D44 is not weakened — traced in vendored source, the light half calls the same `k256::SecretKey::from_slice` on the same locked version, so acceptance is identical **by construction**; lane δ's agreement test survives in its gated module as a drift guard. **Found en route**: `balances()`/`preflight()` were inherent methods on the concrete `AntCoreBackend`, so U14 was un-mockable *with the feature on too*. Consequences: dep-graph gains an additive **rule 5** (one `k256`, pin literal matches, `alloy-signer-local`'s lock entry stays bare); the new ungated module is deliberately **kept out** of the S6 allowlist so the lane enforces that the light half never names upstream

### Due M2
- [x] **D53** State for chain-invalid-at-genTime: `invalid` (recommended) vs `internally-consistent-only` (A9/A18/A21 — "A-OD1") — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D53-chain-invalid-at-gentime.md): the register's **binary framing is overturned**, its recommendation survives for the one sub-case A9 actually means. "A chain that does not validate at genTime" is **six** distinguishable inputs, and MVP-SPEC.md lines 109 and 133 had already ruled two of them the **opposite** way. They partition on a principle new to the tree and shared with D56: **`invalid` iff the artifact makes a claim the verifier can refute from material it already trusts; `internally-consistent-only` iff it makes no refutable and no confirmable claim.** So temporal failure at genTime (**both** directions — the not-yet-valid/back-dating half appears in no spec line, task or matrix entry), a failed path constraint and a failed link signature are `Invalid` **on a chain that names a pinned root**; no pinned root reached (bundle-supplied included) is `InternallyConsistentOnly`. No eighth state, nothing renamed, **zero of the 21 frozen report byte strings move** (measured). Decisive argument was mechanical, not editorial: `MATRIX.json` already pins `anchor-untrusted-root` at that verdict, so the losing option makes a second row re-claim the key — which error-contract §4b refuses. Found the matrix's distinctness check is **dormant** (`"expected": null` on both rows) → Q76; and that the M2 row count is **eight, not seven**
- [x] **D54** Default OTS calendar set (≥2; liveness-verified) (A13/A25 — "A-OD2") — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D54-default-ots-calendars.md): upstream's own four **aggregator aliases**, verbatim and in upstream's order. **The premise handed to the planner was wrong**: `finney.btc.calendar.opentimestamps.org` — the dead host that motivated the question, DNS-unresolvable at 19:16Z and again at 19:34Z — **was never in upstream's default list**; it is a host this project chose. The alias indirection *is* the rot mitigation the decision needed. Success counts **distinct pending-attestation URIs** (a pool and its calendar return the same one — measured 4/4 rounds), ≥2 normal / 1 thin / 0 absent, and never blocks a seal. No calendar key exists to pin and an impersonated calendar can only return a *later* time, so this is liveness, not trust; what **is** pinned is an append-only upgrade-URI host allowlist, closing an SSRF that U24's every-invocation hook would otherwise expose
- [x] **D55** Default Arbitrum RPC endpoint pairs per network (A17 — "A-OD3") — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D55-arbitrum-rpc-pairs.md): `arb1.arbitrum.io/rpc` + `arbitrum.drpc.org`; `sepolia-rollup.arbitrum.io/rpc` + `arbitrum-sepolia.drpc.org`; devnet disabled. **Two measurements decided it that a liveness probe would have missed**: publicnode answers `eth_blockNumber` in 0.75 s then rejects `eth_getTransactionReceipt` (*"Archive requests require a personal token"*), and `1rpc.io/arb` returns `ACAO: *` on the **preflight** and none on the POST, so a browser passes preflight and discards the response. Both survivors return `ACAO: *` on the POST — **D66's Arbitrum half is answered affirmatively today**. "Must agree" is over an **extracted tuple, never bytes**: the two return different key sets (`timeboosted` vs `blobGasUsed`) for an identical tuple. Head-height agreement is not in A17's comparison and could not be (5 endpoints, 3 blocks apart within 0.878 s)
- [x] **D56** Reconcile spec lines 133 vs 108/168 on the OTS `internally-consistent-only` trigger (mismatch → `invalid`; align R wording) (A18/A21/R18 — "A-OD4") — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D56-ots-internally-consistent-trigger.md): the conflict is **real** and the register's line numbers are right, but *"mismatch → `invalid`"* is half an answer. 108/168 win over 133 — yet deleting 133's OTS clause would make the state **OTS-unreachable**, and it is not: its true trigger is 133's own unedited definition, an `.ots` whose ops **do** commit `anchor_digest` while every branch ends in something the verifier cannot evaluate. The **parenthetical** is corrected, not the definition. Decisive argument: the `.sealproof` bundle is **UNSIGNED** and §8 records duplicate artifacts as legal v1, so under refutation-wins appending one garbage branch turns an honest anchor `invalid` — a downgrade-to-forgery-accusation primitive any relay could exercise. Ruled **best-evidence-wins**, which concedes nothing: the forgeable ceiling is `attested`, already non-headline-eligible. Two spec silences closed — `--online` unreachable **or disagreeing** stays `attested`, enforced **structurally** (`OnlineEvidence` has no failure variant), and it gains a `NoSuchBlock` arm without which refutation case O7 is unrepresentable
- [x] **D57** Pinned root-store scope: defaults only vs + documented alternates (recommended: include alternates) (A6/A7 — "A-OD5") — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D57-tsa-root-store-scope.md): include the alternates — **lean confirmed on a reason the register never gave**. The decider is the *sealing* side: U26 makes alternates configurable and A20 aborts on zero *verifying* tokens, so documenting an alternate whose root we withhold ships a **pre-payment abort**, not a weaker bundle. The list is replaced by a closure rule; store v1 = 5 roots / 7 937 B. **Found the finding that would have shipped a broken production default**: two of five real chains (DigiCert→Assured ID, Sectigo→USERTrust; SPKI equality proven byte-wise) terminate at a **cross-certificate**, so a path builder that walks the supplied chain to its end anchors on an unpinned cert and renders **the DigiCert default** `internally-consistent-only`. A24's single-hierarchy test CA never cross-signs — **no synthetic fixture can reach it**. Rulings P1 (stop at first pinned match) / P2 / P3 added to A9. Three of five roots still have an incomplete provenance chain and **no root may be compiled in ahead of its provenance**
- [x] **D58** `opentimestamps` 0.2.0 viability on stable + wasm32; vendor/fork fallback (A11/~~P18~~ — "A-OD6") — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D58-opentimestamps-viability.md): **the crate is adopted in NO form** — not pinned, not wrapped, not vendored. `antseal-core` gets an in-house `.ots` codec over the already-pinned `sha2 =0.11.0` at **+0 packages** against the crate's +13. **Both halves of the register overturned**: it *does* compile (native and wasm32, check and full codegen), so the prescribed test decides nothing; and vendoring is not the cheaper contingency — the crate is **edition 2015** on `std::io::Read`, so every line needing change is a line, and what survives is ~20 lines of protocol constants. What condemned it was executed: an **80-byte `.ots` → uncatchable `SIGABRT`** (`vec![0; attacker_varint]`, 549 755 813 887 B allocation failure — `handle_alloc_error`, so `catch_unwind` cannot help and wasm traps); a **102-byte** second abort (nothing caps the running value); an **87-byte** case that panics in debug and **parses to a different answer in release**, structurally invisible because CI has no release lane; and a **90-byte** case where the crate and `python-opentimestamps` report **different attestation sets** — sealer-authored bytes showing different anchors to different verifiers. Seven limits set with F4 rows; the 3-calendar merge round-trips byte-identically. **P18 is retired** (its whole deliverable was the pin)
- [x] **D59** Request-nonce persistence semantics (capture-time check; bundles carry no nonce) — sign off (A8/A10 — "A-OD7") — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D59-request-nonce-persistence.md): the register's conclusion survives; **its stated reason does not**. 8 bytes, OS CSPRNG in `antseal-anchor`, never derived from `W`; compared in `antseal-core` **only when supplied**; persisted in the vault; **verdict-inert on every bundle path** — with `expected_nonce = None` the nonce is parsed and ignored and may not move any state, code, or byte of report v1. A bundle-side nonce field is ruled a **permanent non-rule for all future versions**. The decisive fact was measured, not read: a real FreeTSA response carries the nonce `0x52E89C47FC426054` **at offset 169 of 4 642 bytes, inside the signed `TSTInfo`** — so *"bundles carry no nonce"* is true of registry keys and **false of bytes**; what the verifier lacks is the second copy, which reframes the residual risk as "the comparison is unwitnessed". Also found: **this host's clock is 129 s slow**, so the obvious `gen_time <= fetch_date` check would reject every committed fixture → now a normative prohibition with a tripwire (A32)
- [x] **D60** DER/CMS/X.509/signature crate pins; P-256 TSA support beyond normative P-384+RSA? (A5/A8/A9 — "A-OD8") — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D60-der-cms-x509-pins.md): seven exact pins into `antseal-core` — `der =0.8.1`, `const-oid =0.10.2` (`db`), `x509-cert =0.3.0`, `cms =0.3.0-pre.2`, `p384 =0.14.0`, `rsa =0.10.0-rc.18`, `sha1 =0.11.0`, plus `oid` on `sha2 =0.11.0`. **55 → 82 packages, zero new duplicate pairs, zero getrandom/rand, wasm32 green.** **P-256 is out of v1** — not on cost: of nine live TSAs captured, exactly one uses ECDSA and it is P-384; **zero serve P-256**. Register overturned substantially: **`rsa 0.9.10` is structurally impossible**, pulling `rand`/`rand_chacha` into core's normal graph, which the dep-graph regex fails red on (+8 duplicate pairs incl. a second `signature`, `digest`, `der`); **`cms 0.2.3` forks the DER stack**, giving a second `x509-cert` — two non-interconvertible `Certificate` types *in the crate that decides which certificate signed a timestamp*; and the nominee list was incomplete (`sha1` is **required**, because ESSCertID **v1**'s `certHash` is SHA-1 by definition). **Nearly shipped a wrong rule and caught it**: `to_der(from_der(x)) == x` is vacuous at the top level (`ContentInfo.content` is an `Any`) and where it is not vacuous it **rejects 6 of 9 real TSAs including DigiCert**, which emit the `certificates` SET in chain order, not DER-sorted order. Two pins are **pre-release** → P23 owns the stabilisation watch and the one mandatory `deny.toml` ignore (verified RED without it)
- [x] **D61** Long-run fuzz venue: nightly CI vs OSS-Fuzz (Q9) — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D61-fuzz-venue.md): scheduled CI (`fuzz-nightly.yml`), **twice weekly** (`41 3 * * 1,4`) at 600 s/target, a named 700 min/month ceiling, and a `scripts/ci-lanes.sh fuzz-budget` guard that goes red when a new target breaks it. **The register's framing is overturned twice.** OSS-Fuzz is **not applicable** — its criterion is *adoption*, not visibility, so Q65's public flip alone does not unblock it; ClusterFuzzLite (a third venue the register never names) is rejected. But **the venue was never the binding question**: the committed daily × 4 × 900 s configuration is a lower bound of **1 824 min/month = 91 % of the 2 000-minute Free allowance**, and Q17's incoming M2 targets take it to **137 %** — past which GitHub blocks **every** workflow in the repository, not just fuzzing. Also found the lane is **live and unobserved**: no record of any `fuzz-long` run exists in `docs/ci-verification.md`, the document whose own rule is that a lane which has never run on the remote is not evidence
- [x] **D90** *(minted 2026-08-02, mid-round)* Anchor network substrate: HTTP-client pin, execution model, and dependency-graph placement (A3; consumed by A10/A13/A14/A15/A16/A17/A24) — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D90-anchor-http-substrate.md). **It did not exist until the M2 wave started**: `tasks/A.md` A3's `Deps` line read *"P: workspace scaffold + HTTP-client crate pin"* and **no such P task existed**, no decision covered it, and every network client in M2 sits on it. Ruled `ureq = "=3.3.0"` (`default-features = false`, `features = ["rustls"]`), **blocking and UNGATED**, declared by `antseal-anchor` alone — default `--workspace` graph **129 → 144 names**, `ant-backend` 447 → 451. No runtime is created, borrowed or required: the frozen `AnchorGate::run` `async fn` keeps its signature with a blocking body (measured: completes in **1 poll** under the project's own noop-waker executor), and endpoint independence comes from `std::thread::scope`. **Both halves of A3's implied lean overturned**: `reqwest` is in the *lock*, not the default graph — ungated it costs **+64…+72** against ureq's +15, and its `blocking` feature (tokio/sync + a background runtime) costs *more* than its async client; and no P task should own the pin, since nothing consensus-affecting flows through it. **Gating behind `ant-backend` refused on D89's ground, measured**: required CI is `--all-targets --locked` with no `--features` and `crates/antseal-anchor/` is absent from `gate-features.sh:88-95`, so every Accept row of A3/A10/A13/A16/A17 — all local-stub unit tests — would have been **compiled by nothing**. Three `ureq` behaviours no documentation states: `.limit(N)` accepts **N−1** bytes (A28's `≤` silently becomes `<`), `max_redirects(0)` **returns** the 3xx, and `Config::default()` reads `HTTP_PROXY`/`ALL_PROXY` — **which would collapse A16's must-agree pair onto one origin**. Transport asymmetry settled and owned (A49): `timestamp.digicert.com:443` **refuses connections** (measured), so plain HTTP is required for RFC 3161 (signed, nonce-bound) while HTTPS is mandatory for esplora/RPC (unsigned — transport is their only integrity control)

- [x] **D91** *(minted 2026-08-02, mid-wave)* The A-domain error-code namespace: one `anchor-` prefix vs per-surface `ots-`/`tsa-`; and the single canonical code for the `.ots` digest-commitment check (A38/Q80, A5, A11) — **Resolved 2026-08-02** (docs/decisions/D91-anchor-error-code-namespace.md): **one prefix, `anchor-`**; the split is rejected; the digest check is **`anchor-ots-digest-mismatch`** and `ots-ops-do-not-commit-anchor-digest` is never minted; D58's sixteen `ots-*` codes become `anchor-ots-*`. Raised by the **A2 lane**, which found that two decisions resolved the *same day* specified different prefixes (D53 §7 vs D58 §10.4) and two different codes for one equality — permanent, because codes are append-only. **D58's own prediction was tested and failed the same day**: §10.4 said *"A5 will want a sibling `tsa-` on the same grounds"*, and A5's decision **is** D60, which opens with `anchor-`. Four resolved decisions choose `anchor-` (D53/D56/D59/D60, 16 codes) against D58's one (16 codes). **The question was never two-way**: seven already-ruled codes belong to *neither* namespace, so the split's honest count is **four** prefixes. The F precedent cuts against it — D78 makes `manifest-`/`bundle-` an information-flow guarantee enforced in the type system, whereas `ots-`/`tsa-` merely re-encodes `AnchorKind`, already a typed field on `AnchorVerdict` *and* on frozen report v1. **The finding underneath**: §2's headline rule — "a domain never mints a code under another domain's prefix" — **is enforced by nothing**, since a foreign-prefix code is distinct, well-shaped and new, so all three distinctness layers and Q52's snapshot pass it while `census` absorbs it into the `(unprefixed)` bucket. That is how sixteen codes came to be specified under an unregistered namespace with a fully green suite → **Q77**. Also settled: **D56 rule O2 is unreachable as written** and names an outcome, not a second check — `anchor_digest` is a parser parameter, so the comparison lives inside `parse_ots` and `OtsArtifact` has no `stamped_digest` field to read

- [x] **D92** *(minted 2026-08-06, mid-round)* What does a `proven` OTS anchor contribute to the anchor-independence count — the calendar set, the Bitcoin block, or the chain? (A67/A40; consumed by A20, R17, U22) — **Resolved 2026-08-06** (docs/decisions/D92-proven-ots-anchor-identity.md): **the chain** — one opaque `AnchorIdentity::BitcoinChain` shared by every headline-eligible OTS anchor whatever its height or calendars, `OtsCalendars` **deleted**. The recorded risk was right and understated it twice: the calendar set is not merely an over-count, it is a **sealer-chosen, bundle-recorded string** — the exact input A40's own `TsaSigner` rustdoc forbids — and it is **provably invariant across `pending → attested → proven`**, because `antseal-anchor`'s merge is a pure insertion that *enforces* attestation retention (`ots/upgrade.rs:356-358`); and the over-count needs **no adversary**, since the measured 2026-08-03 cycle put one digest's three calendars in **three different blocks** (960767/960768/960771), which both the calendar key and the block key score as **3** where MVP-SPEC.md line 19's own model ("OpenTimestamps (Bitcoin) + ≥2 TSAs") scores **1**. The block key fails for a second reason: which height reaches the D79 upgrade group is `merged.added.first()` (`ots/engine.rs:451-458`), i.e. **merge order, not evidence**. D54 §4 already ruled calendars are not witnesses at all (*"calendar compromise is a denial-of-service, not a forgery"*; no key exists to pin). Two heights of one chain are **one identity, two data points** — same PoW, same reorg, same must-agree esplora pair, joint failure. "No identity for OTS" rejected on a different ground: it would break `count == 0 ⟺ UNANCHORED` and make the verifier say nothing was verified about something it verified. Also ruled: **A20's gate must never read the mixed scalar** (A40's `Do` instructs exactly that, and a lone `proven` OTS would clear a `>= 1` test whose spec says "≥1 TSA token" — D54 §3: *"OTS contributes exactly zero to A20's minimum-anchor gate"*), so a kind-scoped `distinct_verified_identities_of(AnchorKind)` is added and A40's `Do` amended. Three findings underneath: the eligibility filter has **no OTS-side witness** (every test that can see it goes through the TSA path); the one existing OTS identity test **cannot distinguish three of the four candidates**, so A40's OTS half has never been pinned by a falsifiable row; and `antseal-core` holds **two disagreeing TSA identity notions** — A31's `(issuer, serial)` vs A40's `subject` DN — of which the **seal-side one over-counts** (a key rotation reads as two TSAs) and is the one U22 prints → **A72**. Errs toward under-counting, stated: registry §8's irreversibility is about the *format*; here it is the **published claim** that cannot be retracted. Zero wire bytes, zero vectors, zero codes; contained to `verdicts.rs` — the count has **no consumer in the tree today** and `evaluate_anchors` is still called only from its own tests

- [x] **D93** Reconcile the M2 anchor tamper matrix with the state machine that shipped: what the forged-header row's outcome must be, whether `verdict:invalid` stays claimed once, and whether the fix is the fixture, the registry or the code (A21/A18/A80/Q75) — **Resolved 2026-08-06** (docs/decisions/D93-online-refutation-precedence.md): the registry cell stands (`verdict:invalid`, claimed exactly once) and **the code is wrong** — D56 §5 put O4 above O6/O7, making both online refutations unreachable whenever the ops commit the embedded header, so a self-consistent forged header renders `attested` against any evidence and an `.ots` claiming a height past the chain tip renders `attested` for ever, which is the defect D56 §3 states verbatim as O7's reason; fix is one guard (O4 fires only when no agreed evidence refutes the height), O5 keeps precedence so §4's anti-downgrade ruling holds; row 4's fixture is load-bearing in two directions (single-branch AND ops-committing) and carries a vacuity latch; D56 §5/§9/§1 and D53 §8 amended; zero frozen report bytes, zero codes minted
