## Joint/shared tasks (implement once — do not duplicate)

- **P10 ↔ F1** — one CBOR-crate evaluation+pin (P records the pin; F owns the technical evaluation + implementation).
- **P11/P12 ↔ C11** — C11's probe produces the evidence; P lands the pins.
- **P9 ↔ S1** — P9 re-verifies/lands the pin; S1 surveys the API surface. Both M0-start.
- **S15 ↔ R11** — one persistence primitive (S15 implements in antseal-net; R11 is the verify-side wrapper).
- **P19 ↔ Q36** — one weekly upstream-check job (P mechanics, Q operation).
- **P13 ↔ Q10** — one advisory lane (P stands it up; Q operates it permanently + licenses via Q29).
- **U31 ↔ Q20** — one copy lint (U owns CLI catalog, Q owns repo-wide guide; single lint implementation).
- **G19/C17/F15/A21/R7-R8 → Q7/Q8** — rows are domain-owned; the harness + completeness registry are Q's. Error-code names must be globally distinct — coordinate through Q7's contract before writing rows.

## Risk watchlist (spec §Risks → owning tasks)

| Risk | Standing mitigation tasks |
|---|---|
| ant-core churn (weekly-to-biweekly) | P7, P9, S2 (boundary), S20, P19/Q36 |
| Autonomi network youth / data-reset history | evidence never depends on storage: R5 layering, A-anchors; single mainnet exposure only at Q34 |
| Receipt not independently verifiable in MVP | A19 classification, S7 capture completeness (no reseal ever needed) |
| OTS pending window / calendar death; TSA churn + cert expiry | A20 gate, A15/U24 opportunistic upgrades, U25 nags, A6/A7/A26 pinned roots, A9 genTime LTV |
| PQC crate maturity (ml-dsa unaudited, 2 advisories) | P12, P13/Q10, C11 probe, C14 fallback slot |
| Vault loss AND theft | U6 KDF+AAD, U7 floor, U8 wrap, U12 export, U18 nags, Q24 docs, Q21 threat model |
| Wallet linkability | receipt opt-in (R13/U29), U10 sub-key, Q25 hygiene doc, Q21 |
| Malicious verifier host | R25 reproducible build + hash, R26 canonical URL, Q30 signing, footer + CLI cross-check |
| Hostile bundles (adversary CBOR/DER/.ots in the browser) | F3/F11 strict+caps, F17/A23/R10 fuzz, A5 strict DER, Q9 lanes |
| Sealer-as-adversary | R3/R4 structural invariants, R7 rows, C7/G11 by-construction guards, subordinate claimed time (R17/R18) |
| Free-TSA terms/rate limits | A10 spacing config, A24 mock-only CI, Q16 policy, Q26 caveats doc |

## Out of MVP scope — v1.1 parking lot (do not implement; capture obligations already covered)

Committed v1.1: arbitrary byte-range reveal *tooling* (`reveal --range` selection + rendering UX — the proof format + verification already ship in MVP via G12/G13; F8 reserves the bundle slots) · Arbitrum-receipt verification chain (capture is complete via S7 — no reseal will be needed) · heading-aware text splitting · grapheme-selection UX.

Registered by the 2026-08-01 M1 planning round: large-blob (> 4 MiB) storage path (D32/D35 record the revisit triggers) · `vault gc` cache prune + an everything-included export variant (D43/D47) · `--max-cost` non-interactive spend ceiling (D36 residual).

Later: eIDAS-qualified tier · hosted SaaS/org gateway · GUI/desktop app · C2PA export · Arweave mirror tier · ZK position-blinded reveals · key escrow/social recovery · re-anchoring service · public anchor-bundle uploads · external-signer wallets.

Scope discipline: if a task seems to require any of the above, stop and check the spec's Parking lot — the MVP obligation is only that these remain *possible* (reserved slots, complete capture), never that they ship.

## Change log

- 2026-08-01/02 — **M1 Storage: planning round + five implementation waves + the gate.** Planning-first per the house protocol: **5 parallel planning agents resolved all 21 open M1 decisions (D32–D52)** into records before any implementation, then **D89** was raised and resolved mid-phase when a blocker turned out to be a product question rather than a coding one. Implementation ran as staged worktree lanes, two at a time on the 2-core host, with a full battery at every merge. **58 M1 rows landed**; counts 320 → 350 tasks, 66 → 67 resolved decisions. Gate at the merge head: `e2e-devnet PASS, 4 suites, 772 s, 14 nodes` + **1764 tests** + fmt/clippy/wasm32/`--all-targets`/dep-graph/secret-guard/golden-vectors/tamper-matrix/traceability/ci-shell.
  - **The planning round earned its cost — the evidence overturned the register's own lean five times.** **D35**: no antseal crate depends on `self_encryption` at all; the register's binary (direct dep vs vendored subset) contained no acceptable answer, because the crate is wasm32-dead on mandatory `tokio`/`rayon`/`tempfile` *before* its GPL licence is even considered, and D32's BLAKE3-256 address rule removes the need. **D33**: block-number capture belongs in `antseal-net::pay()` (the receipt is awaited there anyway), not in the anchor crate the spec comment implied. **D37**: `Vec<TxHash>` is insufficient — finalize needs a quote→tx *map* and per-blob proof records. **D52**: a required *local* gate beats a per-PR CI job, since branch protection is 403-blocked on this plan and a 20th required context cannot be enforced. **D89**: the wallet primitives move into the default graph (measured **120 → 129** packages, zero new lock entries) because feature-gating `init` instead fails on **coverage** — required CI is default-features-only, so four of U11's six Accept rows would have been compiled by nothing, and cfg-inactive code is not even type-checked.
  - **The invariant matrices and the gate suites caught what review would not have.** S16 found a **real double-payment defect** on its first run: resume decided whether money had moved by reading the journal's *state tag*, but D37's capture hook writes the receipt **inside** `pay`, so a crash in that window left a durable receipt under a pre-pay tag and the resume paid again. Fixed by making the journaled receipt the authority. **S31** then found the durability seam had **no implementation** — the only production sink dropped the receipt — and proved the cost live: a SIGKILL after 2 of 5 sub-batch txs stranded **23437500000000000** atto-ANT and re-bought all five (**82031250000000000 spent vs 58593750000000000 accounted**). **S36** found that even after S31, a real `antseal seal` had the **pre-S31 behaviour** while the suites carried the guarantee. Also caught before shipping: a **`--dry-run` that would have paid and uploaded** over a resumable work (it fell into the resume path, which has no truncation point), and an export that dropped the encrypted manifest's address so an **imported vault could never restore** (S29 — and the recorded one-line fix was itself off by one against its own diagnosis).
  - **Two gates were broken in the same invisible way.** `secret-guard` and the devnet gate were **mutually exclusive**: following the documented devnet instructions wrote `.devnet/env`, which turned a required lane red, greenable only by tearing the devnet down — precisely how a genuine leak would also have been explained away. And **the entire M1 devnet suite had not compiled since `b775e02`** (a path D89's wallet move made private), invisible because the gate command builds lib+bin only while the env-gated suites *skip* rather than fail — **a suite that cannot compile and a suite correctly skipped produce identical output**. This is the Q66 traceability shape from the M0 freeze one level down; U39 now requires that lane to be `--all-targets`. Q15's registry was built against exactly this class at the *existence* level (its `pending` rows are checked in both directions) and did not reach the *compile* level.
  - **The dominant defect class held**: recorded guarantees the code does not implement — the pattern the 2026-07-31 review named. M1 hit it five more times (the S27→S31→S36 chain, S29, U8's export expectation, D37's proof-age citation, `SealReceipts`' wrong task ids). **Q69** now machine-checks the rustdoc instance and earned itself immediately: three dangling test pointers, one created by F53 **one commit earlier**. Its recognition threshold was *measured* against the repo's 1 207 test names, not guessed, and the table is in the test's own docs.
  - **Upstream findings, both recorded at source.** D37 cited a ~24 h node-side `QUOTE_MAX_AGE_SECS`; it does **not exist** in the pinned `ant-node 0.15.0` and the single-node path antseal uses reads **no timestamp at any step** — the only real expiry is a 7-day merkle constant on a path D37 excludes, and the ~24 h figure is client-side policy in a cache antseal does not use. D37 amended with the residual **inverted** (the hazard is that there is no expiry), and since a nonexistent constant cannot be asserted, the test pins the merkle constant as a watchpoint (→ S25/S26). Separately, the live Sepolia preflight found the deployed payment-vault bytecode **differs** from what the local devnet deploys (the token matches byte-for-byte), making one of D38's recorded residual risks concrete.
  - **Ops.** The devnet is **feasible on this host and far cheaper than estimated**: in-process nodes (no `antnode` binary), 14 nodes stable in ~6 s, first release build **12m45s** against a 45–90 min estimate. The ~600-package lockfile event landed as one deliberate P7 review with coupled upstream drifts precise-pinned to ant-core's tested set. One integration defect existed **only on the merged tree** (a test double predating `balances()` joining the trait — each lane compiled alone). Two contention artefacts were recorded rather than suppressed: R54's linear-time guard is load-sensitive (→ **R60**) and a journal enumeration races a concurrent `begin()` (→ **S34**). A credit exhaustion killed two lanes mid-exploration with zero commits and zero loss — the read-narrowly-commit-early rule held.

- 2026-08-02 — **M1 delta lane (m1-s36): connecting the durability guarantee to the product.** **S36** + **U40** (attach the durable receipt sink), the **S16** D37 matrix-row correction, **S32** (arm the devnet gate's anti-rot latch). The theme is one the 2026-07-31 review named as this project's dominant defect class — *recorded claims the code does not implement* — caught three times in one lane, at three different distances from the code.
  1. **The sink was not "forgettable", it had already been forgotten.** S31 landed D37 Decision 2's per-sub-batch durability and proved it live; S36 recorded the residual as an obligation, "not a live defect". By then it *was* one: U13's `seal` wiring, landed the same day, built an in-memory `SealReceipts` for the backend and a bare journal for the pipeline, so from `08e501d` a real `antseal seal` had the **pre-S31** behaviour while the suites had the guarantee. Fixed by `SealSession` — the vault is `Arc`-owned **at the point of unlock**, taken by value, so the `'static` sink shares the one `UnlockedVault` (`!Clone`, one `VaultKey`, one zeroize, pinned by a `strong_count` row) and `run_seal` has no expression that builds a sinkless journal. Two ungated scans pin `VaultJournal::new(` **and** `VaultReceiptSink::new(` to one file each; the second closes what the type layer cannot see (a second sink means the backend fires an object nobody armed). Both proven red. A `PayingJournal` bound was rejected **on evidence**: the red-direction rows that prove the sink does anything are built out of exactly the pairing such a bound would make unrepresentable.
  2. **A test double outlived the mechanism it modelled.** S16's D37 row asserted 3 txs for a two-sub-batch work — honest before S31, and still passing after, because `CapturingBackend` journaled only from the success path. Strengthened to **2** rather than renamed: "no quote hash paid twice" is strictly weaker and holds in the pre-S31 tree, where a resume re-buys the same *blobs* under fresh hashes. The double is now the production object (`Arc<VaultReceiptSink>` + a sink-attached journal), so the file has one model of D37's hook instead of two.
  3. **A gate whose own message told you to arm it.** S32 flipped `--require-suites` to the default with `--allow-pending` as the deliberate opt-out. The predicted "one probe argument" was not enough: the probe the entry called sufficient passes the flag **explicitly**, so it would survive a reverted default — the one regression the latch exists to catch. A third probe now runs with no flag at all.
  **Found at base, and it is the most expensive finding of the lane: the entire M1 devnet gate had not compiled since `b775e02`.** `tests/common/devnet.rs` names `antseal_net::evm::WalletKey`, valid when S17 wrote it and demoted to a private `use` by U37/D89 when the wallet light half moved into the default graph. Nothing caught it because the gate command in use (`cargo check -p antseal-cli --features ant-backend`) builds **lib + bin only**, and the devnet suites are environment-gated so they *skip* rather than fail — S6-S8, S17, S18 and S19 were all unbuildable on the mainline from `b11be2f`, i.e. the required storage gate could not have run at all. Fixed here (one import); **U39** gains it as a second and more expensive instance and the reason its lane must be `--all-targets`. Operational note: the first `local-up` of the session died at node 9 ("Failed to create transport") over residue from an earlier run; `scripts/devnet/local-reset` — which exists for exactly that — cleared it, and the boot after it was clean.
  **Live evidence** (14-node devnet, Anvil 1.5.1, chain 31337), both directions through `run_seal` with the resume a **second** `seal` over the same argv so D45 detection is part of the proof: green — child pid 874670 SIGKILLed **inside `pay`** after 2/5 sub-batch txs (state `Anchored`, partial receipt durable = 23437500000000000 atto-ANT, reaped in 5 ms), resume bought **only the 3 remaining**, **5 txs on Anvil = one per sub-batch**, tx_map 5 entries, delta 58593750000000000 atto-ANT **once**; red — same invocation with a sink the pipeline never arms: 23437500000000000 stranded, all 5 bought again, **82031250000000000 spent vs 58593750000000000 accounted**. And the literal pre-S36 tree measured: reverting `seal_run.rs`'s one line makes the green row fail on its **first** assertion. `e2e_kill_resume` 9 → **11 tests / 259.9 s**; **full registry gate PASS, 4 suites, 499 s**. Gate at the lane head: fmt · clippy `-D warnings` · **1764 tests** · `--features ant-backend --all-targets` check · secret-guard · dep-graph · `e2e-devnet --self-test` (7 planted faults), all green. **No new task ids minted** — S38/S39 stay reserved and unspent; the one finding folded into the existing U39. Counts unchanged at 325; S36, U40 and S32 move to done.
- 2026-08-02 — **M1 UX lane (m1-ux): the remaining seal-side user experience and the keyfile wrap.** **U16** (`--dry-run`'s residual rows), **U15** (the three seal warnings), **U17** (the abandonment half), **U18**+**U34** (the export nag and the vault-global slot it needed), **U8** (the keyfile wrap). Five decisions taken and argued rather than deferred, three of them overturning a recorded expectation:
  1. **U8's "D47's export carries the keyfile factor unchanged" is overturned.** A v1 export is encrypted under the passphrase **alone** — `vault/export.rs`'s own format block says "the passphrase … becomes the backup's only key" — so writing one from a two-factor vault would silently produce a one-factor backup, the same silent downgrade U12 refused on the import side, arriving from the other direction. Both directions now refuse and say why; making the export two-factor is recorded as a D47 format event.
  2. **U34 landed rather than degrading U18 honestly**, because the alternative was a nag that can never be satisfied — and a warning a user learns to skip is expensive two screens later at the permanence gate. New record class 6, `store/bookkeeping`, D47 payload key 5, format version unchanged (pre-release, writer and reader move together). One rule worth reading twice: an **import** always ends with at least one recorded export, because consuming an export file *is* the proof a backup existed.
  3. **U17's abandon engine landed; its surface token did not**, and deliberately: D45's own residual-risk section calls the CLI verb-or-flag "a surface addition to be decided deliberately, not smuggled in here", and `cli.rs` + the frozen help snapshot were outside this lane's containment (**U41**). What *was* fixed is that both D45 errors had been instructing users to "abandon it first" with no command behind the words — they now name only exits this build can perform.
  4. **U15's warnings render inside the U14 consent report, not from a second emitter.** The U13 lane had just paid for two layers disagreeing about pre-consent copy; `SealConsent::new` now takes the whole `SealPlan` so the displayed files and the warnings cannot come from two sources. The fine-tree threshold is argued against `MAX_CHUNK_SIZE` (1 MiB = a quarter of the 4 MiB per-blob cap) rather than against intuition, because a threshold near the ceiling would be unreachable for exactly the single-unit binary file where `--no-fine-tree` is the lever.
  5. **The keyfile path rides in the vault header as an optional v1 key**, not in `config.toml` and not only in the environment: the header is read first at unlock and is AAD for every record, so a tampered path breaks authentication for the whole vault instead of silently redirecting at an attacker's file. What that discloses (where the second factor lives) is stated in the module docs and in `docs/vault-keyfile.md`, and recording it is optional.
  **Found**: `ResumeSafetyReason::SourceChanged` is constructed by nothing — the *stronger* outcome, since S11 made resume never open a source file, but a frozen never-constructed class is R57's question (→ **U42**); and D50's "no keystore crate in the workspace graph" was already false before U8 (`keyring` is transitive through `ant-quic` under the `ant-backend` feature), so the assertion was narrowed to what is true (→ **U43**). Error universe gains two vault-band classes: **18 `vault-keyfile-missing`**, **19 `vault-wrap-mode-unsupported`**. Gate at the lane head: fmt · clippy `-D warnings` · **1770 tests** · `--features ant-backend` check · secret-guard · dep-graph, all green; `cli-surface.help.txt` untouched throughout (no flag was added anywhere). Counts 322 → 325.
- 2026-08-02 — **M1 `seal` lane (m1-seal): the last gate-clause work.** **U13** `seal` orchestration, **U14** the permanence-consent gate, **U21** the secret-hygiene harness — the three M1 gate clauses that were still open at the CLI. Both `--no-anchor`×mainnet layers now proven (the CLI *constructs* `SealError::NoAnchorOnMainnet` rather than paraphrasing it, so they cannot drift); D46's three classes and D45's whole matching table are implemented and tested, D46 asserted to refuse **string-identically** with and without `--dry-run`. Two defects were caught by pre-commit review and both would have been expensive — a `--dry-run` over a resumable work fell into the paying `Pipeline::resume`, and the merged consent render keyed on a flag that reads `false` for the commonest resume — so both are now pinned by regression tests. Two decisions taken and argued rather than deferred: `--no-fine-tree` gets a documented `*`/`?` segment-scoped matcher with **no new dependency** (the previous lane's exact-path recommendation is its metacharacter-free case) plus a hard error on a zero-match pattern, and `ConsentRequest` was **left untouched** — the file list, byte totals and balances are per-invocation facts a `ConsentHook` may hold itself, so U14 landed with zero edits inside `pipeline/` and no merge hazard with the concurrent S31 lane. U21 drives 54 real invocations against a sentinel vault and measures `/proc/<pid>/{cmdline,environ}` on a live process, with four self-tests proving the scanner can fail. **Found at base**: `cargo check --features ant-backend` was red at `b11be2f` and nothing was watching (→ **U39**); D37's hook cannot be journal-backed as the seam stands (→ **U40**, S27's remaining half). `--dry-run` is substantially built as a side effect of D49's truncation being a pipeline *prefix*; U16's remaining Accept rows recorded on its row. Counts 320 → 322.

- 2026-07-28 — **M0 wave 7**, the freeze wave, ending at the **`format-v1-freeze` tag** (planning-first: 2 planning agents resolved **D8** and audited the gate → 7 parallel worktree lanes → rolling integration → the gate). Completed **28 tasks**: F4, F18, F20, F22–F25, F30, F33, G24, C27, R33, R36, R37, R41, R42, Q38, Q40, Q41, Q43, Q49–Q53, Q57, Q58, **Q14**. Froze **D8, D17, D29, D30**. Discovered **21 tasks**. Gate at the tag: **1221 tests** (1113 at wave-6 close), fmt/clippy/wasm32/bit-match over 12 vectors/vector freeze/**registry freeze digest**/tamper matrix COMPLETE/cross-check 0 discrepancies/traceability all-M0-covered/ci-shell/ci-lanes.
  - **The gate audit found the gate itself unrunnable, and proved it rather than arguing it.** Q14's `Accept` required both `--freeze-boundary` green *and* every normative row ticked — but rows N1/N2 sit **inside** the `FREEZE-BOUNDARY` markers, which are byte-compared against D84 §7, so `- [ ]` → `- [x]` was drift. Demonstrated on a scratch copy. **Q49** blanks the marker on both sides and bounds the tolerance with four self-test cases: a tick passes, a changed word *or a deleted marker* still goes red.
  - **Two freezes turned out to be declarations with no mechanism.** **D30**'s append-only code rule was enforced by review discipline alone — the three existing layers check distinctness and shape, neither of which a rename violates, and **53 of 191 codes (28 %) could be renamed with a fully green suite** (**Q52**, validated live: renaming one produced exactly one failure across 706 tests). And the **wire registry had no freeze digest at all** — the 19 D10 caps were pinned only by "code and JSON agree", which a coordinated two-file edit satisfies forever (**Q50**). A third: the pinned CBOR encoder is item 1 of Q14's own `Do` and was **asserted by no test** (**Q53**).
  - **D8 removed wire surface rather than confirming it.** `tsa_anchor.source` leaves v1 — zero producers, zero consumers, the only rendered `tstr` neither signed nor committed, and the only assigned key with no spec line. It also found `block_number` **under-determined** (multi-tx payments span blocks, so two honest sealers emit different bytes) and **D82 absent from the registry entirely**. F4 then moved 33 sections, 30 of them substantive — exactly D8's prediction.
  - **Every lane found its own task text wrong, and in the expensive direction more than once.** R36/R37's incidence rule said a leaf-level cover node appears iff `n` is odd; it needs an odd **unit boundary**, and acting on the stale rule yields a fixture that discloses nothing and the false conclusion that the class does not exist. F20's Accept was unachievable because F8 had since made those states unrepresentable. F18's premise was half stale — two of its four rows existed, and adding them would have collided. Q38's key regex would have failed 20 of 34 committed keys at a gate where the tempting fix is the bytes. F22 miscounted its own scope; F25's "recursion is already depth-bounded" was false (the bound is in a *private* method).
  - **F30's real numbers were worse than recorded, and its own test was vacuous.** The `files` site amplifies **157×** (3.1 MB from a 20 KB input), not 32× — fuzzing had simply reached the sigmap case first. Worse, every allocation fixture ended at its container head, so `remaining()` was **zero** at the allocation, and a mis-united clamp is indistinguishable from a correct one when one operand is zero.
  - **Reverse coverage earned itself immediately, twice.** F23 found **88 of 95** `bundle-`/`manifest-` codes unaccounted on the first run, 65 of them with no owner at all (**F39**). F24 found `cbor-int-out-of-range` is not merely unrowed but **unreachable** — no v1 schema slot decodes through `i64()`.
  - **The independent cross-check rejected three new fixtures and was right all three times** — a count-cap fixture that was truncated as well as over-cap (so which fault fired was an ordering accident), and two whose layer was invisible to the checker's descent. A second implementation catching three defects in the first, in the wave that freezes both.
  - **R36 did not go red as predicted — it generalised**, and the generalisation was proved: all 32 bytes of a real leaf-level payload flipped, rejection **class** asserted per half. The `88 + 67` authentication boundary still holds exactly, which is the property D83 option B was chosen to preserve.
  - **Ops findings.** Three integration defects existed **only on the merged tree**, all from D8's one deletion landing in a different lane from its three users — source code, an assertion's grep target, and committed fixture bytes — and none was visible to git, because no two lanes touched the same file. A fourth was avoided: lanes C and E both moved `bundle.json`, and the merged file's digest matched **neither** lane's `FROZEN.sha256` entry, so resolving that conflict by picking a side would have frozen a manifest that does not describe its own file; regenerating from the emitter reproduced git's merge byte-for-byte. Worktrees were placed **outside** the repo this wave, which structurally removed wave 6's `.cargo/config.toml` doubling. The machine has **2 cores**, so lanes were staged 4 → 2 → 1 rather than run all at once; wave 6's "agents stalled at ~600 s having produced nothing" is very likely the same constraint. Pre-allocated ID blocks per lane eliminated wave 6's three ID collisions entirely.
  - **Not pushed** *at the wave's close* — superseded the same day: the local reflog records the freeze push (`5f794ff → d3345e1`, 21:27Z), see the 2026-07-31 entry. `docs/ci-verification.md` carried **two contradictory** authoritative context sets (17 names omitting `traceability`, and a later "still 16"); the real set is **19 from 17 jobs** (this line originally said 18 — corrected 2026-07-31, after b8411e5's recount), and a branch-protection payload cut from any stale copy would have left required lanes unprotected.

  - **Three decisions overturned or corrected their own register entries.** D81 was recorded recommending a cause discriminator; the analysis found the premise backwards — the AEAD is not the binding mechanism for either mutation, both are caught independently at stage 4, and the two candidate discriminators (the BLAKE3 chunk address, speculative re-decryption) each break something worse. D76 confirmed its lean on entirely different grounds: the recorded reason (a new HKDF label) costs almost nothing pre-Q14, while the real arguments are a D78 presence-tier drop, a doubled permanent code surface, and the fact that **the split does not remove the leak** because the mirror's `true_length` already publishes raw size. D77's recorded shape was already closed by F5; the hole that actually survived was the **mirror-only file**, which no record had named.
  - **Findings that change future work**: C21 found the HKDF/HMAC key state is un-zeroizable on the current pins — a live contradiction of spec line 143 (→ C22). R9 found `REPORT_VERSION` 0 → 1 rewrites all 21 pinned report byte strings and must precede `status frozen` (→ R32), a coupling Q14's checklist does not name. G20's proof-mutation property found that a leaf-level cover seed's upper 16 bytes are never examined, so `.sealproof` bytes are not unique for a proof (→ D83/G23). R10 measured the M0 authentication boundary at exactly 88 + 67 bytes. F15 established that a wrapped codec rejection surfaces its inner code unchanged at every layer, so canonicality classes are many fixtures and one code — fixtures pin `(code, layer)` rather than forking the taxonomy per layer.
  - **Ops findings**: **every single agent worktree this wave was cut from a stale base** (`c85d963`, 71 commits behind) — not the occasional hazard wave 3 suggested, but the default; all 13 self-corrected only because the base check was mandatory in every brief. A new failure mode appeared: 4 of the first 8 agents hit a stream-watchdog stall at ~600 s having produced nothing, all recovered by resuming from transcript with an instruction to read-narrowly-and-commit-early. Worktrees sit *inside* the repo, so cargo merges both `.cargo/config.toml` files and the wasm runner doubles — `CARGO_TARGET_WASM32_UNKNOWN_UNKNOWN_RUNNER` is the workaround. Three ID collisions had to be reconciled at merge (two lanes each minting G21, R31/R32, F19/F20) — parallel agents cannot see each other's numbering. Two integration defects were visible only on the merged tree: F10's roster still listed G15's landed vector as pending, and an orchestrator conflict resolution collapsed two `#[cfg]`-gated module declarations under one attribute, un-gating R7's slice and breaking the wasm32 tier.

Completed: F5/F6/F7, C10/C12/C13/C14, G3/G4/G5/G8, R3, Q7. Resolved D9, D15, D22, D23, D24; D17 ratified in code (freeze Q14); D30 formalized as docs/testing/error-code-contract.md.   - **Notable findings**: G3's independent Python oracle agreed with the pinned Rust crate on **every** fixture at first run — including all eight invalid-UTF-8 classes, so CPython's and Rust's decoders demonstrably implement the same §3.9 maximal-subparts rule D20 froze; NFC oracle safety across differing Unicode versions rests on the Normalization Stability Policy (fixtures confined to scalars assigned ≤ 6.0). C13 needs **no** pre-validation layer (C11's finding held up in implementation) but must never touch the crate's `Signer`/`Verifier` traits, which sign with the empty ctx. C14 found that `SigPolicyUnknownAlg` is only reachable through a wire-id entry point, since `SigAlg` cannot represent an unregistered id — hence `SigPolicy::from_ids`. D24 turned out to be expressible as a type witness rather than a runtime check. R3 needed a sortedness pre-pass so a descending unit table reports `Unsorted` instead of a misleading first-range `Gap`, and `RawMirrorInTilingSet` got a precise trigger (non-mirror ranges fail, but including the mirror's would tile). Two registries now describe the same algorithm-id assignment (C's semantic, F's wire) — pinned together by test; delegation is a future cleanup.
  - **Ops findings**: agent worktrees were correctly based this wave (the wave-2 stale-base hazard did not recur). Two process failures worth not repeating: the first launch of all six agents died together when usage credits ran out — nothing was lost only because they had committed incrementally — and later a broad `pkill -f "worktree-agent"` by the orchestrator matched the agents' own process command lines and killed all three mid-task. Incremental commits again limited the loss to one placeholder KAT digest and two unrun gates. **Rules for next wave**: never pattern-kill on a string that appears in agent paths, and keep the per-task commit instruction in every agent brief.
- 2026-07-27 — v1.1: adversarial verification applied. Coverage audit: **0 gaps** against the spec. Consistency audit: 12 defect groups fixed — R16/R21 redefined as library APIs (U28/U30 are the sole CLI owners of `reveal`/`verify`); A18 cedes verdict aggregation to R17; C17↔R7 tamper-fixture ownership resolved (R owns committed fixtures, C owns primitives+helpers); wallet keygen/import/address ops added to S5 (was an orphaned U11 dependency); A6→A7 sequenced; U13⇄U14 and eight consumer-reference cycles annotated (Deps-discipline rule 7 added); A5/A11/A12 real-fixture bootstrap via early A25 capture noted; multi-OS corpus lane added to Q1 (G3 was unexecutable); D31 scoped against D12.
